# L3 — Library: Agents & Atlas — Implementation Plan (built; revision 3 as shipped, see the Revision log at the end)

> **Revision 2 (2026-10-27).** Written against `main` at `b6b221a` (L1, M3, M4a, the UI redesign PRs #32–#42 and the dev-loop
> infrastructure are merged) **and against `l2-library-curation.md` revision 3** (migration `0010`, contracts C118–C132, decisions
> D-L2-1…43, registry 109 → 126), which is *not built yet*: every L2 name used here is taken from that document and each lane re-reads the
> merged L2 code first (a rename is recorded in this plan in the same commit). The previous text is kept beside this file as
> `l3-library-agents-atlas.draft.md` for diffing. Every claim in "Baseline reconciliation" was checked in the worktree
> `metamusic-wt/l2` (code, migrations, `docs/plans/`, `web/src/`, `scripts/`, `.github/`); nothing was checked against a running system.

   **109** operations on main (the draft assumed 116), **126** after L2 rev 3, **133** after L3 (126 + 7).
2. **Cross-references fixed.** Every reference to the L2 *draft* is mapped to L2 rev 3 **by heading and content** (table A.6 below; the map is a uniform +16:
   draft C102 packages → C118, C104 Records → C120, C105 slots/edits → C121, C106 changelog/`ChangeText` → C122, C108 liner/recordview → C124, C109 Crates → C125,

3. **Baseline reconciliation rewritten** (about 70 rows: *exists as assumed / differs / missing / provided by L2 rev 3*). The consequential findings and their resolutions:
   - **`sounds` already owns the Song "subject" rule** (`loadSubject`: Pick take, else newest non-discarded take, else draft, else none) and computes the Song's collapsed tempo
     (`collapseTempo`, `TempoDetail`). `features` must not invent a second rule: F1 exports it as `sounds.SubjectOf` (behaviour-preserving) and `song_features` uses it
     (`subject = none` ⇒ no row). The draft's discarded-take fallback is **dropped** (D-L3-3, D-L3-39).
   - **`sounds.Service.Map` owns its transactions** (version insert commits, then the recompute runs in separate batched transactions) — the draft's "one write transaction, `Map`
     included" and "`Map` upserts `provenance_dirty`" are false. TP1 splits `Map` into `applyTx` + `recompute` (behaviour preserving); `Decide` writes the version **and** the proposal
     row in one transaction and then recomputes; **features are refreshed inside each recompute batch by an injected hook** (no queue rows, no wait for the sweep) (D-L3-41, C140.3).
   - **`song_sounds.ord`** is the position of the row in the emitted row list (unique per Song, no ties), not "first phrase index with ties"; the compute definition enters
     **`songDigest` only** (`mm-sound-compute/2`), not the take digest (D-L3-9).
   - **Ears names differ**: the estimator is `analysis.ComputeTempo(x, rate)` (not `EstimateTempo(PCM)`), `TierA.Tempo` exists with its own argv test, `earstest` is one file.
     E1 adds `ffmpeg.Mono`, `analysis.ComputeKey/ComputeEnergy` and `TierA.Mono`; `(*FFmpeg).Tempo` stays as a thin wrapper so L1's argv/tempo tests keep their expectations (D-L3-8).
   - **`api.SongFilterIn` already is the shared filter struct** (`internal/api/types_l1.go`, converters `songFilter` and `soundSearchFilter`); no `songs/filter_input.go`. The Atlas op embeds it,
     and — exactly as `list_songs` — **the handler expands `crate_id` through L2's `crates` service** before the filter reaches `atlas` (D-L3-44).
   - **`TestMuseText_OnlyInAllowlistedOps` is the `museTextOps` variable of `internal/api/ops_l1_leaks_test.go`** (8 ops on main, +`get_record` from L2) and **no `TestMuseReaders` exists**: the L3
     reader guard is a new AST-scan test, and the allowlist edit is one named line owned by INT-B.
   - **The landing page is `New` (`/`), not Home**; `home` is the Library list (`/library`). The nav badge is the **inbox** (`needsYouCount()` = approvals + open duels + Pick proposals,
     built from limit-1 lists). **`get_stats.attention` is dropped** (D-L3-33): L2's `get_stats.curation.records.proposed`, `list_taxonomy_proposals(state=open, limit=1).total` and the existing counts feed the same badge,
     which also removes a hot-spot edit of `library/stats.go`/`ops_stats.go`.
   - **`propose_taxonomy` has no UI** (a human maps terms directly with `map_sound_term`): its `UI` is `none:agent-face (…)`, exactly like `propose_pick` — so the "agent does every UI action"
     set gains **5** entries, not 6 (D-L3-38).
   - **`record_sequences.strategy` loses its `CHECK`** (validated in Go): a new strategy is "one target curve and one weight row" (extension move 4), which a SQL CHECK would turn into a table rebuild (D-L3-40).
   - **`provenance` may not import `features`** (L1's direct-import table); the step interface returns a provenance-local `FeatureChanges`.
   - **`FixtureL3` is SQL-only, with literal derived feature rows** (independent oracle for the `features` lanes), signature `FixtureL3(tb, st, now)` like `FixtureL1`/`FixtureL2`.

>
> **Binding lessons carried from M2b/M2c/M3/L1/M4a/L2 and the dev-loop work:** never mutate process globals (`slog.SetDefault` etc.) in `run()` or handlers; e2e specs must not depend on counters other specs change (each worker has its own server, but a spec file shares it); startup waits in tests tolerate
> instrumented (`-cover`) runs (generous deadline, readiness poll, never a fixed sleep); new tests are safe under `t.Parallel()` and `-shuffle` (no shared buffers, loggers or globals); a derived-state writer is idempotent and digest-guarded wherever more than one entry point may run it (D-M3-3, D-L1-4: the Reconciler and the `Map` recompute hook both call
> `features.ComputeTx`, D-L3-3); a perf test never hard-codes a wall-clock literal (`MM_PERF_FACTOR`) and is named `TestPerf_*` (it runs in the CI perf job, not the race shards); a spec file starts with a `// matrix:` header; Muse text never leaves the app except by an explicit operator opt-in (D-L1-6, D-L2-16);
> the privacy gate is public-repo grade (no hostnames, personal names, lyrics or balances in any tracked file); anything that must be identical on amd64 and arm64 is computed in integers (D-L2-9 cover renderer pattern), which is why the sequencer's costs and the Atlas layout are fixed-point (D-L3-12, D-L3-29).

**Goal:** make the library *explorable* and *assistable*. L1 made it self-organising (Songs, Muses, Picks, Sounds), L2 made it curatable (Records, Crates, tape). L3 adds the third thing the operator asked for in "categories and
lists": a **position** for every Song. Each Song gets measured-or-estimated **features** (tempo, energy, key, each with its basis), and four capabilities are built on them with strict REST/MCP/SPA parity: (1) **sequencing** — an
agent or a human orders a Record by tempo, key and energy with a per-step explanation, and an agent **proposes** the Record (a human publishes); (2) **Muse-aware next actions** — `/context` and a new `list_next_actions` tell an
agent what the library needs ("Muse X has 3 Songs, none rendered — estimate the first"); (3) **taxonomy proposals** — agents propose Sound-term mappings with an impact preview, a human accepts; (4) the **Song Atlas** —
a 2-D map of the whole library (tempo × energy, genre family × vocal, requested BPM × measured BPM, or any pair) with Muse and Sound overlays, which also **absorbs the M6 "requested vs got" layer**. **Nothing in L3 spends
money; every hint that mentions generation names a free estimate operation and the M3 confirm protocol stays the only money path.**

**Architecture:** one new migration (`0011_agents_atlas.sql`: the derived caches `take_features` and `song_features`, the append-only `record_sequences` and `taxonomy_proposals`, one added column on `song_sounds`, one queue trigger and a
queue re-seed) and five new Go packages (plus additions to the existing `sounds`, `records`, `recordview`, `muses`, `provenance`, `analysis`, `ears`, `songview` and `trackctx`) with a one-way dependency order: `internal/features` (feature derivation, the
Reconciler's third step, the energy prior, Camelot and key parsing, `Verify`), `internal/sequencer` (pure: integer costs, exact search ≤ 12 Songs and a bounded heuristic above, the explanation), `internal/sequence` (feature loading and
the `suggest_sequence` service), `internal/nextactions` (the rule engine), `internal/atlas` (axes registry, fixed-point layout, query, adherence). The existing `sounds` gains proposals and impact and an atomic split of `Map`; `records` gains the
`sequence` slot op; `analysis`/`ears` gain two Tier A analyzers `key` and `energy` over **one** shared decode; `trackctx` gains a nil-safe reader interface. 7 new registry operations (2 MCP tools, 4 generic, 1 excluded; registry 126 → 133)
feed REST, MCP and four new SPA surfaces, all **in-app routes of the shipped shell**: the `/atlas` Library sub-view, a Sequence tab in the Record studio (`/records/:id`), a Proposals tab in `/sounds`, and a "What next" section on the New page (`/`).
Backfill is not a program: the migration re-seeds the L1 reconcile queue and the same Reconciler drains it.

**Tech stack:** Go 1.27 (stdlib first; no new dependency), Huma v2, modernc SQLite, the existing ffmpeg (decode only), Svelte 5 runes + TypeScript strict + Bun/Vite, Playwright, an HTML canvas for the Atlas (no charting dependency). No new
Go module, npm package, binary or config variable.

---

## Why this model (design thinking, public-safe)

**Where L1 and L2 stopped, and the question L3 answers.** L1 answered "how was this made, and which take is best?"
(provenance). L2 answered "what do I do with it?" (curation: Records, Crates, tape). Both are *membership* answers: a
Song is in a Muse, in a Record, in a Crate, under a Sound facet. L3 answers a different question — **"where is this
Song, and what goes next to it?"** — which is a *position* answer. Position is the missing third kind of category:

| Kind of grouping | Question | Mechanism | Time behaviour | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Membership** (hand-made list) | "which Songs are *in* this?" | Crate, Record, Playlist, Tag | static until a human edits | L2 / M2a |
| **Predicate** (computed filter) | "which Songs *match* this?" | Sound facets, Muse, Session, smart Crate | recomputed on every read | L1 / L2 |
| **Position** (computed geometry) | "which Songs are *near* this, and what does the whole library *look like*?" | **features → Atlas, neighbours** | recomputed when a measurement changes | **L3** |
| **Sequence** (optimised order) | "in what order should these Songs be heard?" | **sequencer → a Record's order, with reasons** | computed on request, then it is just a Record edit | **L3** |

This is why the *album* is "surpassed" and not deleted: its living half is the Record (L2), its frozen half is the Tape
(L2), and the thing people actually did with albums — **choosing which songs belong together and in what order** — is
exactly what a position map and a sequencer make cheap. The Atlas turns "select a region of the map" into "make a Crate"
or "make a Record from these", and the sequencer turns "these six Songs" into "an order I can defend". The extended
decision rule of the library (L2's, with the two new moves added):

> If a grouping can be **computed** it is a filter. If it can be **positioned** it is the map. If it needs a **human
> decision about membership** it is a list. If it needs a human decision about **order and release** it is a Record —
> and *ordering* may be proposed by a machine with its reasons attached.

**Agents propose, humans decide — one pattern, now complete.** The library has three kinds of act: *working data* that
anyone with write scope may change (Crates, tags, Muse assignment, comments), *judgments* that move what everyone sees
(the Pick, the taxonomy), and *release* acts that move what leaves the app (publishing a Record, exporting a tape).
L1 gave judgments their first proposal flow (`propose_pick` / `pin_pick`), L2 gave release its (`propose_record` /
`publish_record`). L3 closes the pattern for the last judgment that agents could still change directly — the **Sound
taxonomy** (`map_sound_term` was agent-writable in L1, with a note that "L3 adds proposals with human acceptance"):

| Act | Agent face (write scope) | Human face | Why the split |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pick | `propose_pick` | `pin_pick` / `dismiss_pick_proposal` (`/api/`, `ActsAsHuman`) | a Pick drives Records and playback |
| Record | `propose_record` (now optionally **sequenced**) | `publish_record` (`/human/api/`, `KindHuman`) | release: leaves the app |
| Sound taxonomy | `propose_taxonomy` (with an **impact preview**) | `decide_taxonomy_proposal` (`/api/`, `ActsAsHuman`), `map_sound_term` | changes every browse count and every smart Crate |
| working data | direct | direct | reversible, local, no one else's view changes |

**What an agent is for here.** The concept's agents (§4) read the library, judge with what they can measure (they cannot
hear: `perception.blind_spots`), and propose. Three gaps remained: they had no *entry point* ("what should I do next?"
was answered only per take, with five generic hints), no *ordering tool* (L2 deliberately left sequencing to L3), and no
*way to see the library whole*. L3 supplies those three — and nothing that makes them autonomous: no LLM runs inside the
server, no suggestion is acted on without a call a human can see in the audit log, and the next-action rules are
deterministic functions of library state (a suggestion disappears when the condition is resolved; there is no
"dismissed" state to drift).

**Honesty about estimates is a feature.** Real libraries are mostly *unrendered*: at planning time the library is ~450
draft Songs with a requested tempo and ~13 rendered recordings with none of the measurements L3 needs. A map that only
plots measured Songs would be empty; a map that silently mixes predictions and measurements would lie. So every feature
value carries a **basis** (`measured · declared · plan · predicted`), the Atlas draws predicted points hollow and reports
how many points rest on each basis, and the sequencer lowers a component's weight in proportion to how few Songs have
it. Energy is explicitly **relative** inside an ordering (rescaled to the Songs being ordered), because an absolute
"energy" from a few signal measures is not trustworthy across genres — the arc is about *shape*, not level.

**Four extension moves, so that "categories in the future" never mean a new subsystem.** (1) a new *feature*: one entry
in `features` plus one analyzer or prior (M5's Tier B tempo/key and M6's engines plug in as new bases); (2) a new *axis*:
one entry in the Atlas axis registry plus a golden row; (3) a new *next-action rule*: one rule struct, one golden row;
(4) a new *sequencing strategy*: one target curve and one weight row. Each is a code change with a table test — never a
new table, route or op.

## Dependencies and sequencing

- **Hard:** M0–M3, **L1**, **M4a** and the UI redesign and dev-loop work as merged on `main` (`b6b221a`), and **L2 revision 3 merged and green** (its INT-B, INT-W and X1 done: records, slots, the changelog, `recordview`, Crates and the filter vocabulary, `FixtureL2`).
  Every L3 lane branches from that `main`. Schema version after L3: **11** (`0011_agents_atlas.sql`; L1's `0008_provenance.sql`, M4a's `0009_remote.sql` and L2's `0010_curation.sql` are the previous three). A database at schema 5–10 migrates the missing steps and 0011 in one boot;
  0011 writes only queue rows (no feature, proposal or sequence row). L2's optional lanes A2/S2 (Muse auto-albums) are **not** a dependency. Work that touches no L2 code (E1 Ears, the pure parts of FC1/SQ1/AT1) may be prototyped on a scratch branch earlier but is merged only after `0010` exists, because F1 owns `0011`.
- **What L3 uses (binding; each item verified in the baseline tables):**
  - **L1:** `songs` (live/hidden/retired, `retired_into`), `song_roots` and the `take_songs` view, `song_picks` (the Pick take is a Song's *subject take*) and `pick_events`, `song_sounds`/`take_sounds`/`sound_state`/`song_unmapped_terms`, `sounds.Service` (`Index`, `Map`, `Taxonomy`, `ComputeTx`, `SweepStaleTx`) and the package functions
    `Parse`, `CompareTempo(requested, measured *float64) (mismatch, octave bool)`, `TempoBand(bpm float64)`, the `TempoDetail` of the collapsed `tempo_band` row, `songs.ListFilter`/`songs.FilterSQL(f, excludeFacet)`, `api.SongFilterIn`, `store.SongStateSQL`, `muses.Service.Refs` (exists; batch),
    `songview` (`MuseSummaries`), `provenance` (the queue `provenance_dirty`, `Settle`, `Sweep`, phases A/B, `Verify`, the `PickStep`/`SoundStep` interfaces), the Ears `tempo` analyzer (`analysis.ComputeTempo`, `TempoDef`), `api.RefFor`, `Principal.ActsAsHuman()` (human or paired device), `store.Env`, `store.ParseInstant`, `store.FormatTime`, `store.Querier`.
  - **L2 rev 3:** `records.Service` (`Create`, `EditSlots`, `Propose`, `Resolved`, `Changes`, `Verify`), `records.SlotOp`/`ResolveSlot`/`ChangeText`/`WhyText`, the tables `records`, `record_slots`, `record_changes` (`detail_json`, `slot_moved`, `proposed`), `recordview` (`Get`, `GetOptions.Include`), `crates.Service`
    (`ExpandSongs`) and the vocabulary `mm.crate-filter/1` (L2 adds the Song predicates `unheard`, `min_duration_s`, `min_stars`, `in_record`, `crate_id`, `pick_decided`, …), `curation` settings, the ops `create_record`, `edit_record_slots`, `propose_record`, `get_record`, `list_records`, `FixtureL2` (SQL-only).
  - **M1/M2a:** `tracks` (`bpm_declared INTEGER`, `duration_s REAL NOT NULL`, `recorded_at`, `status`), `scores` (`bpm REAL`, `bars`, `seconds_per_bar REAL`; **no key**), `recipes.request_json` / `recipe_drafts.request_json` (`style`, inline `abc`), `analyses` (`loudness`, `tempo`; PK `(track_id, analyzer, version)`, immutable), `ratings`,
    `trackctx` (`Build`, `next_actions`, `Fit`, `maxNextActions = 5`), `get_stats`, the New/Library/Sounds/Song/Muse/Board routes.
  - **M2b/M2c:** `ears.AnalyzeTrack`/`Backfill` and the Tier A skip rule, `earstest` fakes, `yue2.StyleTerms`, `BlindSpotFacts`, reference and lossy takes (first-class in every L3 structure, D-L1-23).
  - **M3:** `estimate_generation` (`mode: from_draft`, `draft_ids ≤ 50`), `estimate_variant`, `create_duel` — named in next-action hints, never called by the server.
  - **M4a:** connector principals are `KindAgent` (`Via oauth`, never `ActsAsHuman()`); Subsonic keys are `KindDevice` principals that authenticate only inside `/rest/`; nothing in L3 is exposed to Subsonic.
- **Operator inputs (tracked in X2/X3):** approval of the agent refusal on `map_sound_term` (D-L3-22), of Muse text in `list_next_actions` (D-L3-16), of the Atlas showing *predicted* points (D-L3-14), of the absorption of the M6 SA track (D-L3-27), of the default sequencing strategy `arc` (D-L3-11),
  of the Atlas as a Library sub-view (D-L3-37); a spot check of measured **key** and **energy** on three FLAC reference recordings; review of the first Atlas, of the first agent-built Record and of the first taxonomy proposals. No secrets, keys or spend.
- **Approvals and gating (P.5).** The *fallback is the default* until a box is ticked, and a box blocks only the lane that depends on it. **F1 is gated by two boxes** (below), because F1 is the irreversible step (the migration and the one-time recompute of every Song's Sound rows); F2 is gated by D-L3-22 only as far as the frozen `Meta` of
  `map_sound_term` is concerned, and F3 by D-L3-37:

  | Box | Default until ticked | Blocks (only) |
  |---|---|---|
  | **D-L3-1 scope and vocabulary** (features, sequencing, next actions, taxonomy proposals, Atlas; nothing spends; the Ears lane separable) | none: **F1 does not start without it** | **F1** (and so everything) |
  | **D-L3-9 `song_sounds.ord` and the one-time recompute** (every Song's Sound rows are rewritten once after the upgrade) | none: **F1 does not start without it** | **F1** |
  | D-L3-22 (`map_sound_term` refused to agents; **explicit approval**: it also removes agent taxonomy edits that concept §5 allows) | agents keep `map_sound_term` (`generic`), parity case kept, `propose_taxonomy` is an optional gentler path | F2's `Meta` change of `map_sound_term`, the guard and its three `TestMap_*` tests (TP2) and the parity/UI-action −1 (INT-B) |
  | D-L3-16 (Muse titles in `list_next_actions`) | item texts say `Muse <first 8 chars of the id>`; the Muse-text allowlist is **unchanged** | the Muse-title variant of `Names.Muse` and the allowlist +1 (NA1, INT-B) |
  | D-L3-37 (Atlas is a Library sub-view) | as designed; moving it is a one-line change in `nav.ts` | **F3** only |
  | D-L3-14 (Atlas shows predicted points) | the SPA's **Hide predicted points** toggle defaults ON; the API is unchanged | nothing on the backend |
  | D-L3-27 (M6 SA track absorbed) | M6 draft untouched | only the M6/roadmap edits in X1 |
  | D-L3-21 (decide on `/api/` with a runtime check) | as designed (`pin_pick` precedent) | nothing; recorded for acknowledgement |

  Every lane other than F1 starts without any other box.
- **Timing:** nothing date-bound. L3 must merge before the M5/M6 drafts are renumbered; their X1 tasks read the real highest contract and migration numbers from `docs/plans/` at that time instead of assuming a literal.

### Contract numbering and the later drafts

L2 rev 3 ends at **C132** and migration **0010**. L3 takes **C133–C146** and **0011**. The M5/M6 drafts (written before M3 took C68–C86 and before M4a took C102–C117) still claim old ranges; their X1 renumber tasks read the real highest numbers at the time and therefore start **after C146 and 0011**.
X1 appends that sentence to the M5 and M6 draft headers and makes the M6 edits of D-L3-27 (the M4a draft is built and needs none). Decision ids `D-L3-1…44`; test files added by L3 are suffixed `_l3`.

Status legend: **OK** = exists as the draft assumes; **DIFF** = exists differently (the contract is corrected); **MISSING** = does not exist (L3 builds it); **L2** = provided by L2 revision 3 (not built yet; taken from that document). Method: `ls`/`grep`/`go doc` over `internal/`, `cmd/`,
`web/`, `scripts/`, `.github/`; `internal/store/migrations/*.sql`; `openapi.json`; `docs/plans/*.md` headings; the L2 rev 3 text. `go build ./...` is green on the worktree. Nothing was run against production, a container or the network.

### F. Overlaps and conflicts: how each is resolved

1. **L3 vs `sounds` (subject, `Map`, digest, `ord`)** — one subject rule exported; `Map` split atomically; `ord`/`compute_def` as B.4/B.5; `sounds` files have one owner per lane (F1: `compute.go`/`subject.go`; TP1: `map.go`, `service.go`, `impact.go`, `proposals.go`; TP2: `proposals_decide.go`).
2. **L3 vs L2 (`records`, `recordview`, Crates)** — additive hooks only: no L2 DDL change, `ProposeWith`, a `sequence` case in the slot-op dispatch, three `ChangeText` rows, `include=sequence`; L2's own tests stay green (`TestProposeRecord_L2Unchanged`).
3. **L3 vs the redesign** — Atlas is a Library sub-view, the Sequence panel a Record tab, Proposals a Sounds tab, What-next a New section; no new tab, no secure page, `playerStore` untouched, dark only (D-L3-37).
4. **L3 vs the dev loop** — lanes iterate with `make verify`; hot-spot files have single owners (C146, "Lanes and tasks").
5. **L3 vs M6** — unchanged from the draft (D-L3-27).
6. **L3 vs L1's import tables** — `provenance` stays free of `features` (local `FeatureChanges`), `songview` reads `song_features` by SQL, `trackctx` reads perception through `ears` types only.

---

## Decisions recorded by this plan

These refine the design of record; X1 copies them into the private design document. Concept, L1 and L2 decisions
are inputs, not repeated.

| Id | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-L3-1 | **Scope.** L3 = the **features layer** (tempo, energy, key, each with a basis), **sequencing** (`suggest_sequence`, the slot op `sequence`, `propose_record.sequence`, durable explanations), **Muse-aware next actions** (`/context` strings, `list_next_actions`), **taxonomy proposals** (`propose_taxonomy`, `list_taxonomy_proposals`, `decide_taxonomy_proposal`), the **Song Atlas** (`get_song_atlas`) and the absorbed **requested-vs-got** layer (`get_adherence`). Nothing spends. Lanes branch from `main` after the L2 merge (D-L3-42); migration 0011. **Not in L3:** learned embeddings or any ML genre/mood classifier, MAP-Elites or "propose for cell" generation (rejected by the M6 judges, stays out), Atlas gap proposals, Atlas bins, saved Atlas views, Tier B (M5) features, per-Song tempo maps, key *changes* within a Song, DJ-style transition effects, autonomous agents or schedulers, any LLM call inside the server, auto-publishing, Subsonic exposure of features. | Concept §7 L3 plus the operator's request to think about categories and lists "now and for the future"; the YAGNI list keeps L3 shippable. |
| D-L3-2 | **Vocabulary.** New words: *feature*, *basis*, *Atlas*, *sequence*, *next action*, *taxonomy proposal*. The eight concept nouns are unchanged and the Atlas is a **view**, not a ninth noun (it has no table of its own). Tables: `take_features`, `song_features`, `record_sequences`, `taxonomy_proposals`. Packages as in C133. The word *album* stays confined to `internal/albums` (D-L2-2; `TestNoAlbumWord_L2` still green). | Concept vocabulary is binding. |
| D-L3-3 | **Features are derived, versioned and idempotent.** `take_features` (per Take) and `song_features` (per Song, from its **subject** — the rule `sounds.SubjectOf` already uses: the Pick take, else the newest non-discarded take, else its draft, else none; D-L3-39) are derived caches written only by the `features` package: the Reconciler's third step `features.Service.ComputeTx` (the D-L1-4 pattern: triggers enqueue, one pass derives, `Settle` drains, the migration re-seeds the queue so backfill is the steady-state path) and, after a taxonomy change, the same `ComputeTx` called by the `sounds` recompute hook inside the recompute transaction (D-L3-41). Both entry points are digest-guarded and idempotent (the pattern `sounds` already follows when `Map` recomputes outside the Reconciler). Both tables are deletable caches (D-L1-24 pattern: `TestNoDeleteSQL` allowlist +2, rebuilt by delete-and-insert); freshness is a per-row `digest` and the definition `mm-features/1`; stale rows rewrite on the startup and hourly sweep. Values are **fixed-point integers** (`bpm_x100`, `energy_x10000`) so that equality, ordering and goldens are exact. | One writer package, no drift, no call-site edits; integers make L3's goldens platform-independent. |
| D-L3-4 | **Basis ordering.** Per value: tempo `measured` (Ears `tempo`) > `declared` (`tracks.bpm_declared`, else the style's BPM term) > `plan` (`scores.bpm`); key `measured` (Ears `key`) > `declared` (a mode in the style text) > `plan` (the `K:` header of the inline ABC); energy `measured` (Ears `energy`) > `predicted` (the prior of D-L3-7). The row stores the chosen value, its basis and — for tempo — both `bpm_requested_x100` (declared else plan) and `bpm_measured_x100` so that the requested-vs-got layer needs no second source. A value is **never invented**: no basis ⇒ NULL. A reference take (stub recipe) has only `measured` bases (D-L1-14 pattern). | Concept §6 honesty; L1 D-L1-14/17 already separate requested from measured. |
| D-L3-5 | **Tempo position rule.** The position tempo of a Take is its measured BPM **unless** the L1 comparison reports an *octave* relation to the requested BPM (`octave: true`, within 6 % of 2× or ½×), in which case the position is the **requested** BPM (basis `declared`/`plan`, `detail.octave = true`): a half/double-time estimate is the likelier error, and the map must not scatter a Song to the wrong half of the axis. The measured value is always kept in `bpm_measured_x100`, so the requested-vs-got view still shows the disagreement. | L1 D-L1-17 treats octave errors as tolerance, not truth; the same stance for position. |
| D-L3-6 | **Key.** Key is a Camelot code (`1A`…`12B`; letter `A` = minor, `B` = major) stored as `(key_num, key_letter)`. Tier A analyzer `key` (`mm-key/1`, C136.1): chroma over 110–2 000 Hz from the shared 11 025 Hz decode (8192-point window and parabolic peak interpolation, so a semitone at 110 Hz spans about 5 bins) with a tuning estimate, correlated with the 24 Krumhansl–Schmuckler profiles; a result needs `r ≥ 0.45` and a margin to the runner-up `≥ 0.04` (**starting values, not yet contract**: E1 step 0 prototypes them on `FixtureL3Audio` and pins or changes them, C136.1), otherwise the key is **null** (recorded with its numbers). The analyzer ships behind the constant `analysis.KeyEnabled` (D-L3-35): the real library has measurable key for at most 13 Songs. A relative major/minor runner-up within 0.05 sets `ambiguous_relative`. It is an *estimate* for a steady tonal centre: modulations, modal and percussion-dominated music are blind spots listed in the dossier. | A key estimate that answers "unknown" when unsure is more useful to a sequencer than a confident wrong one; unknown costs neutral. |
| D-L3-7 | **Energy.** Tier A analyzer `energy` (`mm-energy/1`, C136.2) is a documented composite in [0, 1]: onset density (weight 0.45, 0.5→0 … 5.0→1 onsets/s), mean spectral centroid (0.30, 600→0 … 2 800→1 Hz) and the stored integrated loudness (0.25, −26→0 … −9→1 LUFS; the term is dropped and the others renormalised when no loudness analysis exists). It is an **estimate used relatively**: sequencing rescales energy to the Songs being ordered (`min→0, max→1`; a spread below 0.05 collapses to 0.5), because absolute energy is not comparable across genres. **Predicted energy** (basis `predicted`, `mm-energy-prior/1`, C135.4) is the mean of per-facet means of a committed table over the subject's `mood`, `genre_family` and `tempo_band`, so that drafts can be placed on the map and ordered; it is labelled everywhere and never counted as measured. | The concept's "tempo × energy" needs a per-Song scalar the library does not have; the prior makes the Atlas useful on a library that is mostly unrendered, honestly. |
| D-L3-8 | **One decode, three analyzers.** `ffmpeg.Mono(ctx, workPath, maxS)` (ffmpeg `-ac 1 -ar 11025 -t 240 -f f32le`, the argv of L1 C94 unchanged) returns `analysis.PCM`; `ears.TierA` gains `Mono` and drops `Tempo` from the interface; `(*FFmpeg).Tempo` stays as a wrapper (decode + `ComputeTempo`) so L1's `argv_tempo_test.go` and `tempo_test.go` keep their expectations. `ears.AnalyzeTrack` decodes **once** per Take when **any** of `tempo`, `key`, `energy` is missing (or `Force`) and runs the pure `analysis.ComputeTempo` (unchanged), `ComputeKey` and `ComputeEnergy` on the buffer (each skipped when present, unless `Force`; a failure is a warning, never fails the take). `earstest.TierA` fakes `Mono` deterministically. | Three decodes of a 240 s master would triple the backfill cost and the failure surface; keeping the L1 symbol names avoids churn in merged tests. |
| D-L3-9 | **`song_sounds.ord` and the compute definition.** 0011 adds `song_sounds.ord INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`: the 0-based **position of the row in the emitted row list** of the Song's subject (engine, then style matches in `Parsed.Matches` order, then language-from-lyrics, then tempo rows; after `dedupe`) — unique within a Song, so no tie-break exists. The **primary** value of a facet for a Song is its row with the smallest `ord` (`synthpop` ⇒ pop before electronic). The L1 Song digest (`songDigest`) gains `compute_def = "mm-sound-compute/2"` (take digests are unchanged), so every Song is stale after the upgrade; the migration re-seeds the queue and the Reconciler recomputes each Song once (cheap: it is the L1 backfill path; `SweepStaleTx` with `scanDigests` finds any leftover). `features` copies the five primary values into `song_features` for indexed Atlas queries. **F1 is gated on the operator box for this decision.** | A 2-D map needs one position per Song per categorical axis; "first emitted row wins" is deterministic and explainable. |
| D-L3-10 | **Sequencing is an ordinary, logged Record edit.** `sequence` is a **slot op** of `edit_record_slots` (same principal rules as every slot edit, D-L2-4: any write principal on `draft`/`proposed`, humans only on `published`); it reorders the live slots by the sequencer, writes one `slot_moved` row per slot whose position changed (with `detail {"by":"sequencer","strategy":…,"sequence_id":…}`) and one durable `record_sequences` row holding the explanation (ids and numbers only). `propose_record` takes the same optional `sequence` field and applies it **in the same transaction** before proposing (one revision bump). `suggest_sequence` is the free, read-only preview of exactly what the op would do (same code, `digest` equal to the one the op would store). A sequence never changes *membership*, pins or sides; endpoints (`first_song_id`, `last_song_id`) are the only constraints. | Preview and apply cannot disagree; history stays complete (concept §6: "a Record never silently changes"). |
| D-L3-11 | **Strategies and the objective.** `arc` (default: low → peak at 70 % → soft landing), `ramp_up` (energy ascends), `steady` (smoothest path, no shape). The objective is `J = Σ transitions (w_t·tempo + w_k·key + w_e·energy) + w_s·Σ shape`, all costs integer micro-units in [0, 1 000 000] (C137.2), weights per strategy (C137.3) scaled by the **coverage** of each feature (a feature known for 40 % of the Songs counts 40 %). Search is **exact** (a *suffix* Held–Karp over (remaining set, first of suffix), position-aware, C137.4) up to `ExactMax = 12` Songs and a deterministic **beam + local search** above (≤ 60), gap thresholds pinned by test. Exact ties break by the lexicographically smallest id sequence. **Determinism means: the same ordered input gives the same output.** Only the exact path is also independent of the input order; above 12 Songs the input order seeds one local search, so a different input order may give a different (equally valid) result. The guarantee `cost_output ≤ cost_input` holds only for calls **without endpoints** (C137.4). Missing features cost a neutral 0.5, never zero. | Optimal where cheap, bounded and deterministic elsewhere; exactly reproducible for the explanation. |
| D-L3-12 | **Integer arithmetic in the sequencer** (inputs `bpm_x100`, `energy_x10000`, Camelot ints; every cost and `J` an `int64`; no `float32/float64` identifier in `cost.go` and `plan.go`, `TestSequencer_NoFloatInCost`). Reported costs are `J / 10¹⁰` rounded to 4 decimals. | Goldens identical on amd64 and arm64 (D-L2-9 pattern). |
| D-L3-13 | **Explanations are first-class.** Every step carries machine reasons (`tempo_close`, `tempo_octave`, `key_same`, `key_relative`, `key_adjacent`, `key_boost`, `key_clash`, `key_unknown`, `energy_rise`, `energy_fall`, `energy_hold`, `arc_peak`, `opener`, `closer`, `no_features`) and a rendered sentence; the suggestion reports input vs output cost and a signed `improvement_pct` (null when `cost_input = 0`, negative when endpoints force a worse order than the input), the feature **coverage and bases**, and warnings (`low_coverage`, `predicted_energy`, `heuristic`, `no_features`). The explanation JSON `mm.sequence/1` contains **no titles and no Muse text** (names are injected at read time, the `ChangeText` pattern). | The concept: "sequencing by tempo/key/energy **with explanation**"; the publish page shows why. |
| D-L3-14 | **Atlas = positions, honestly.** `get_song_atlas` returns one point per live Song at fixed-point coordinates in [0, 1]² computed **server-side** (so REST, MCP and the SPA see one map), with, per point, the basis of each plotted axis (`m` measured, `p` predicted, `d` declared, `l` plan) and, per response, how many points rest on each basis. Songs lacking a numeric axis value are **not plotted** (counted in `totals.missing`); on categorical axes they sit in a trailing `(none)` band. Predicted points are drawn hollow by the SPA, and the legend says that predicted energy partly encodes tempo band, genre and mood (C145.2); until the D-L3-14 box is ticked the SPA's "Hide predicted points" toggle defaults ON. | A map that hides how much of it is guesswork is worse than no map. |
| D-L3-15 | **Next actions: one engine, two surfaces.** `nextactions` evaluates a **registry of rules** (C139.2: stable code, priority 0–100, audience, minimum scope, a batch candidate query, a text template, an optional hint naming a registry op). `/context.next_actions` stays a `[]string` (backwards compatible; at most **2** Muse-aware entries, inserted by the priority rule of C139.4); `list_next_actions` returns structured items for the whole library. Rules are pure functions of library state: a suggestion disappears when its condition is resolved; there is **no stored dismissal** and no table. At most 5 items per code are listed, the rest are counted in `groups`. | The concept's example ("Muse X has 3 Songs, none rendered") is Muse-level while `/context` is take-level; a single engine keeps both honest and cheap. |
| D-L3-16 | **Muse text in next actions.** (Until the P.5 box is ticked item texts say `Muse <first 8 characters of the id>` and the allowlist is unchanged.) `list_next_actions` item texts name the Muse **title** (≤ 60 runes, artist omitted), exactly as `/context` already shows the Muse to read-scope agents (D-L1-6); the op joins the L1 allowlist of Muse-text ops (**+1**, enforced by `TestMuseText_OnlyInAllowlistedOps`). The Atlas, `suggest_sequence`, `get_adherence`, taxonomy proposals and sequence explanations carry **ids and numbers only**. Muse text is read only through `muses.Service.Refs` (never SQL) by the one new Muse-text reader, `nextactions`. | The concept says agents see Muses; the structural guards keep every other new surface blind. |
| D-L3-17 | **`/context` priority rule.** Order: the existing base rules (rate, open fixes, untimed, truncated) → up to 2 Muse-aware entries **of priority ≥ `ContextMuseMinPriority = 50`** by descending priority → the generation hint → the critique line (always last). The list stays ≤ 5: Muse-aware entries use only the room left after the base rules and the critique line, and the generation hint keeps its existing condition (`len(na)+2 <= 5` evaluated **after** the Muse-aware entries). Existing goldens are unchanged when a take has no Song/Muse. The floor keeps low-value rules (`song_no_muse` 25, `features_missing` 20, `taxonomy_unmapped` 35, `record_unsequenced` 45) out of `/context`, so they can never displace the M3 generation hint (spend funnel) from the base list; they stay in `list_next_actions`. | Backwards-compatible and bounded; the base critique discipline is not displaced. |
| D-L3-18 | **No money, no execution.** Hints are advisory `{op, args}` objects for **free** operations (`estimate_generation`, `estimate_variant`, `create_duel`, `get_muse`, `suggest_sequence`, `propose_record`, `propose_taxonomy`, `list_songs`); none names a spend operation; a hint is shown only when the caller's scope would allow the op. `TestL3_NoSpendOps` asserts every L3 op has `cost: free`; `TestNextActions_HintsAreFree` asserts every rule hint's op exists in the registry with cost `free`. | CLAUDE.md rule 6; spend stays M3's. |
| D-L3-19 | **Rule catalogue (initial).** `record_proposed_waiting` (90, human), `record_undecided_slots` (80), `muse_record_candidate` (75), `muse_unrendered` (70), `taxonomy_proposals_open` (70, human), `song_duel` (68), `muse_unjudged` (65), `muse_partial_angles` (60), `record_empty_slot` (60), `song_proposals_waiting` (55), `song_tempo_mismatch` (50), `record_unsequenced` (45), `taxonomy_unmapped` (35), `song_no_muse` (25), `features_missing` (20, human audience). Adding a rule is a registry entry plus a golden row (extension move 3). | Concept §4 examples, extended with the states L1/L2 created. |
| D-L3-20 | **Taxonomy proposals.** An agent (non-`ActsAsHuman`, write scope) posts ≤ 50 `{term, facet, value, op}` mappings (the shipped `sounds.MapEntry`) with a rationale (1..500). The server validates them with the shipped `validateMap` rules against the **current effective index**, drops mappings that are already effective (`unchanged`; none left ⇒ 422 `NOTHING_TO_PROPOSE`), snapshots an **impact preview** (Songs that would gain or lose each `(facet, value)`, unmapped terms cleared; ≤ `ImpactMaxSongs = 20 000` Songs, `truncated` flag) and stores the proposal `open` (≤ 10 open per principal, ≤ 50 open overall; an identical open set from the same principal is returned, `reused: true`). A human decides: **accept** (all mappings or a subset by index) re-validates the accepted mappings against the index *now* (mappings that went stale are `skipped`; none applicable ⇒ the proposal becomes `expired`, 409 `TAXONOMY_STALE`), then in **one transaction** inserts one extension version attributed to the human (note `proposal <id>`) through `sounds.applyTx` and marks the proposal `accepted`; the affected Songs are recomputed by the same recompute loop `Map` uses before the call answers, and `song_features` is refreshed inside each recompute batch by the recompute hook (D-L3-41); **reject** closes it. | Concept §7 "taxonomy proposals by agents (human approves)"; L1's own risk note. |
| D-L3-21 | **Who may decide.** `decide_taxonomy_proposal` lives on `/api/` (scope write) with a principal check `ActsAsHuman()` (a human or a paired device), its MCP face is `exclude:human-judgment`; any other principal ⇒ 403 `HUMAN_ONLY` before any read. Taxonomy changes affect browse counts and smart Crates, not what leaves the app, so the light act of D-L1-11 (pin) applies rather than the 2FA path of publishing. **Why `/api/` and not `/human/api/` (CLAUDE.md rule 3):** `/human/api/` is reserved for acts whose effect leaves the app or needs the stronger human path (publish, export, spend); judgments that only change what the library shows follow the `pin_pick` precedent (D-L1-11): `/api/` with a runtime `ActsAsHuman()` check that refuses every non-human principal with 403 before any read, so a paired phone can decide. The deviation is deliberate and recorded in P.5 for acknowledgement; `pin_pick` and `dismiss_pick_proposal` already ship exactly like this on main (`MCP: exclude:human-judgment`, a `UI` route, 403 before any read). | Consistent with the Pick rule; phone-friendly. |
| D-L3-22 | **`map_sound_term` becomes a human act (amends D-L1-15/D-L1-20).** Its MCP face changes from `generic` to `exclude:human-judgment` (so `call_operation` no longer lists it and the registry-derived "agent does every UI action" set skips it); a non-`ActsAsHuman` principal over REST ⇒ 403 `HUMAN_ONLY` with the hint `use propose_taxonomy`. **Needs explicit operator approval** (P.5): it removes a capability L1 gave agents *and* agent taxonomy edits that concept §5 allows. **The fallback is the default until the box is ticked:** agents keep `map_sound_term`, `propose_taxonomy` stays as an optional gentler path, `TestMap_AgentAllowed` stays, and the only change is the extra ops. The guard (C140.4), its tests and the parity/UI-action −1 belong to F2 (the frozen `Meta`), TP2 (guard and tests) and INT-B (parity/UI-action −1) and wait for the box; no other lane depends on it. | An agent-writable taxonomy changes every count the human sees; the proposal flow is the L1 risk note's own resolution. |
| D-L3-23 | **Registry.** 7 operations: 2 MCP tools (`list_next_actions`, `propose_taxonomy`), 4 generic (`suggest_sequence`, `list_taxonomy_proposals`, `get_song_atlas`, `get_adherence`), 1 excluded (`decide_taxonomy_proposal`: `exclude:human-judgment`). Registry **126 → 133** (109 on main + 17 of L2 + 7). Parity cases: **+6** (every op with an MCP face), **−1 only if D-L3-22 is approved** (`map_sound_term` leaves the parity runner), plus the extended arguments of the existing `propose_record` and `edit_record_slots` cases (new rows in L3's own parity files; L2's parity file is not edited). The "agent does every UI action" set gains **5** entries (`propose_taxonomy` has `UI: none:agent-face`, D-L3-38) and loses `map_sound_term` only with D-L3-22. `tools/list`: F2 measures the compact size on top of L2's re-baselined ceiling and sets `toolsCeilingBytes` to the measured value rounded up with a one-line justification (never an `-update` side effect); if the L3 tools alone exceed **4 000 compact bytes** F2 demotes `propose_taxonomy` to generic and re-measures; beyond that it stops and asks the operator (D-M3-36 pattern; the `list_songs` demotion is the precedent). | Bounded MCP surface. |
| D-L3-24 | **Atlas axes are a registry.** Numeric: `tempo` (log₂, 60–200 BPM), `energy` (0–1), `duration` (60–360 s), `bpm_requested`, `bpm_measured` (same scale as `tempo`); categorical: `family`, `vocal`, `era`, `mood`, `language` (primary Sound values, fixed order lists, a trailing `(none)`), `key` (the 24 Camelot codes). Any pair with `x ≠ y` is valid (422 `AXIS_PAIR_INVALID` otherwise). Named presets (SPA buttons, no server concept): `tempo-energy` (default), `family-vocal`, `requested-got` (`bpm_requested` × `bpm_measured` with identity and octave guide lines). | Concept §7 names two pairs; the registry makes the rest free and future axes one entry (extension move 2). |
| D-L3-25 | **Overlays are encodings of the same points.** `color_by` ∈ `none · muse · state · pick · family · vocal · era · mood · language · key · record` (palette index per point + a legend with counts; Muse legend entries are **ids**, names come from `list_muses`); the shared Song filter vocabulary (`muse_id`, `session_id`, `sound`, `state`, `unheard`, `in_record`, `crate_id`, …) either **restricts** the points (default) or, with `dim=true`, flags non-matching points `h:0` and returns all of them. Because `crate_id` is part of the vocabulary, **a smart Crate is a map region** for free. `near_song_id` + `k` (≤ 25) adds the nearest Songs by the sequencer's steady transition cost. | Muse/Sound overlays are required; reuse of the filter struct keeps list, Crate and map in agreement (`TestAtlas_FilterEqualsListSongs`). |
| D-L3-26 | **Atlas selection hands off to existing ops.** The SPA lets the human select points (click, rectangle) and then **Add to Crate…**, **New Record from selection…** (`create_record{song_ids}`, optionally followed by `edit_record_slots{sequence}`), or open `/board`/`/songs/:id`. No Atlas-specific write operation exists. | Architecture rule 1 (a capability exists iff it is a registry op); parity by construction. |
| D-L3-27 | **The M6 Song Atlas track is absorbed.** The requested-vs-got layer is delivered here: the `requested-got` preset, `bpm_requested`/`bpm_measured` axes, `get_adherence` (per-Song and per-Sound-facet-value aggregates of the **Pick take**, with M6's error formula `min_{k∈{½,1,2}} |got·k − requested| / requested · 100`, octave flag, 5 % band, and expected-vs-actual duration), and the `/atlas` route (the page owns both views). M6 drops: @@C138, tasks M6-SA1/SA2, the `m6-atlas` lane and spec, D-M6-17, its Tier A `tempo` analyzer (L1 owns it), `internal/atlas` (name now L3's), `/atlas` as adherence-only, and the "Song Atlas map" bullet of its Out-of-scope list is deleted (the map exists; **MAP-Elites and cell proposals stay out**). M5 Tier B's `tempo_ratio`/`key_relation` are not consumed in L3; when M5/M6 land they add a *basis* to `features` (a derived-cache migration, extension move 1). **Needs the P.5 approval.** | One map instead of two, one tempo analyzer instead of two, the same formulas; the SA track was the smallest and most overlapped M6 item. |
| D-L3-28 | **Atlas aggregates by Sound facet value, not raw style term.** M6 aggregated by the first ≥ 3 raw style terms; L3 aggregates by `(facet, value)` of the Song's primary Sound rows (`n ≥ 3`, `min_n` configurable), because those are the stable, taxonomy-backed categories the Atlas and the Board already use. | Raw style terms are free text (hundreds of near-duplicates); facets are the library's controlled vocabulary. |
| D-L3-29 | **Atlas layout is deterministic and integer-only.** Coordinates are Q16 fixed point rounded to 4 decimals on output. Numeric axes: `u` as in C141.2. Categorical axes: bands in a fixed order, only the categories *present* in scope (plus `(none)` last). Collisions are resolved by a **sunflower (Vogel) spiral** with a committed 360-entry unit-circle table (no `math.Sin/Cos`), grouped by cell: `1/128` cells for numeric×numeric, band×band for categorical×categorical, `1/64` buckets for mixed (a horizontal strip inside the band). The layout is computed over the **whole** scope then paged by id, so pages are mutually consistent; `layout_token` (a hash of axes, filter, `dim` and the in-scope rows themselves, C141.3) in the cursor makes a stale page a 409 `ATLAS_STALE`. `TestAtlas_NoFloatInLayout` greps `layout.go`, `ilog2.go`, `spiral_table.go` and `quant.go` (the one file that converts Q16 to the 4-decimal output; no other file formats a coordinate). | Reproducible maps, stable goldens, and an agent and the SPA see the same coordinates. |
| D-L3-30 | **Atlas size.** Rows (not columnar), title truncated to 60 runes, ≤ `AtlasMaxPage = 600` points per page (default 400 for a human principal, `AgentDefaultPage = 100` for any other principal, so an MCP result is ~12 KB rather than ~50 KB), page cap 90 000 B enforced in Go; scope capped at `AtlasMaxScope = 5 000` Songs (`truncated: true`, newest first, with the count; the SPA says "refine with a filter"). No bins, no cell proposals (D-L3-1). Hidden, retired and archived Songs are excluded as in every list. | The real library fits one page; growth is paged without a second rendering mode. |
| D-L3-31 | **Immutability and write owners.** `record_sequences` and `taxonomy_proposals` are append-only/no-delete (state transitions on proposals only, decided rows immutable); `take_features`/`song_features` are deletable caches owned by `features`; `song_sounds.ord` is written by `sounds`. `TestSQL_WriteOwners_L3` greps non-test Go for writers outside the owner packages. | Rules 5, 7, 8. |
| D-L3-32 | **Eventual consistency, bounded and visible.** Features lag by at most one Reconciler pass (`PollInterval = 1 s`, L1); `get_song_atlas` and `get_song.features` report `pending: true` while any dirty `track`/`draft` key exists for an in-scope Song; the Atlas page refetches (1.5 s, ≤ 3 times) as the Song page does. `suggest_sequence` and the `sequence` op read features **inside their own read/write transaction** and warn (`pending_features`) when keys are queued. | Same trade as D-L1-4/D-L2-31. |
| D-L3-33 | **Needs-you counts without a new field (revised).** The draft's `get_stats.attention` is dropped. The human sees what waits through counts that already exist or are built by other ops: Pick proposals (`list_songs?has_open_proposals=true`, as the inbox does today), proposed Records (`list_records?status=proposed&limit=1`; for agents L2's `get_stats.curation.records.proposed`), open taxonomy proposals (`list_taxonomy_proposals?state=open&limit=1`); the New tab badge (`needsYouCount()`) and the "Needs you" section sum them, each failure keeping the previous number. | Parity by construction (agents read the same lists); no edit of `library/stats.go` or `ops_stats.go` (L2 lane A owns them). |
| D-L3-34 | **Contract numbers** C133–C146, migration 0011 (schema 11), decisions D-L3-1…45, test files suffixed `_l3`; the M5/M6 drafts renumber after C146/0011. | Continuity. |
| D-L3-35 | **Scope valve (revised: demote by default).** The *concept acceptance* is the only blocking scope: **Record sequencing** (planner, `suggest_sequence` for a Record or `song_ids`, the `sequence` op, `propose_record.sequence`), **Muse-aware next actions** (the core rules below), **taxonomy proposals**, and the **Atlas** (tempo × energy and family × vocal with Muse/Sound overlays and the totals/basis honesty layer). Everything else is **non-blocking and ships only if spare**, cut in this order: (1) `near_song_id` neighbours and the Song page's Nearby; (2) `color_by=record`; (3) the `duration` axis and the `key` axis / `color_by=key` (with the key analyzer); (4) `suggest_sequence crate_id`; (5) `get_adherence` by-facet aggregates and the Adherence tab (the `requested-got` preset and the `bpm_requested`/`bpm_measured` axes stay); (6) the **`key` analyzer** (`analysis.KeyEnabled = false`: key stays declared/plan/null, neutral cost, zero weight; also the prototype failing its bar in E1 step 0); (7) the rule catalogue beyond the **core seven** — core: `record_proposed_waiting`, `muse_record_candidate`, `muse_unrendered`, `taxonomy_proposals_open`, `song_duel`, `muse_unjudged`, `muse_partial_angles`; demotable: `record_undecided_slots`, `record_empty_slot`, `song_proposals_waiting`, `song_tempo_mismatch`, `record_unsequenced`, `taxonomy_unmapped`, `song_no_muse`, `features_missing`; (8) the weekly 300-sequence CI job. The **Ears lane** (E1) as a whole is separable: if cut or if the real-music spot check fails, `features` keeps tempo (measured by L1 or declared) and **predicted** energy, key is null, the Atlas still renders (predicted/declared), and no other acceptance item depends on it except `TestAcceptance_L3_AnalyzersOnRealAudio` (media tier). The taxonomy-proposals lane is independent of every other lane; `get_adherence` is independent of the rest of the Atlas. | Keeps L3 shippable on the stable core and says plainly which surface has no acceptance item. |
| D-L3-36 | **Extension moves are the documented way to grow** (feature, axis, rule, strategy); each has a named test that fails when the registry and its golden disagree (`TestFeatures_RegistryGolden`, `TestAtlas_AxesGolden`, `TestNextActions_RulesGolden`, `TestSequencer_StrategiesGolden`). | Future "categories" never mean a new subsystem. |
| D-L3-37 | **Web placement.** The Atlas is a **Library sub-view** (segmented row Takes · Sounds · **Atlas** · Records · Crates · Playlists) at `/atlas`: it is a browse-the-whole-library view whose selection hands off to Crates and Records, which L2 rev 3 already placed in the same row (D-L2-38); `SubNav` scrolls horizontally. The **Sequence** panel is a tab of the Record studio (`/records/:id`) because sequencing is an edit of one Record; **Proposals** is the third tab of `/sounds` (Songs · Terms · Proposals) because a proposal is a taxonomy act; **What next** is a section of the New landing page below "Needs you" (the PWA cold start is where an agent's open work and the operator's pending decisions meet). No new tab, no new secure page (`decide_taxonomy_proposal` is an in-app act like `pin_pick`), no new icon unless a lane needs one. **Gated by a P.5 box that blocks only F3.** | Listen-first shell: browsing in Library, working data inside its object, attention on New. |
| D-L3-38 | **`propose_taxonomy` is an agent-face op.** Its `UI` is `none:agent-face (a human maps terms directly with map_sound_term)` (the `propose_pick` precedent), `Idempotency: create`, `Rate: heavy`; the SPA never calls it. `list_taxonomy_proposals` and `decide_taxonomy_proposal` keep `UI: /sounds`. Consequence: served-operation coverage needs six L3 UI ops served (`suggest_sequence`, `list_next_actions`, `list_taxonomy_proposals`, `decide_taxonomy_proposal`, `get_song_atlas`, `get_adherence`) and the every-UI-action script gains five entries. | Registry rule 1 needs a UI route or a stated reason; shipped precedent. |
| D-L3-39 | **One subject rule.** `song_features` derives from the Song's *subject* exactly as `sounds` does: `sounds.SubjectOf` (F1 exports the existing `loadSubject` logic: Pick take, else newest non-discarded take by `(recorded_at DESC, id DESC)`, else the Song's draft, else none). `subject_kind` is `take` or `draft`; a Song with **no** subject has no `song_features` row (the Atlas counts it as `missing`); a Song that has takes but whose subject fell through to its draft because every take is discarded carries `subject_note = 'all_takes_discarded'`. The draft's "newest discarded take" fallback is dropped. `TestFeatures_SubjectEqualsSounds` asserts agreement on every fixture Song. | Two implementations of "which take represents a Song" would drift. |
| D-L3-40 | **Strategies are validated in Go.** `record_sequences.strategy` has a length CHECK only; `sequencer.Strategies` (a registry pinned by `TestSequencer_StrategiesGolden`) is the allowed set. | Extension move 4 must not need a table rebuild. |
| D-L3-41 | **`Map` is split, features follow the recompute.** TP1 extracts `applyTx(ctx, tx, p, entries, note)` (the version and term inserts) and `recompute(ctx, p)` (the batched loop) from `sounds.Service.Map` without changing behaviour or error codes (`TestMap_*` unchanged); `Map` = validate + `applyTx` in its own transaction + `recompute`. `Decide` runs the re-validation, `applyTx` and the proposal-row update in **one** transaction, commits, then calls `recompute`. `sounds.Service.SetRecomputeHook` (nil-safe; stored by F2, called by TP1, wired by INT-B) runs inside every recompute batch transaction with the Song ids the batch rewrote (`Changes.SongIDs`, additive) and `app` wires it to `features.ComputeTx(ctx, tx, env, nil, songIDs)`, so `song_features`' primary Sound columns change in the same transaction as `song_sounds`. A crash between the commit and the recompute is healed by the startup sweep (SQL-stale by taxonomy version, as for `map_sound_term` today). **Acknowledged in P.5 (it touches shipped taxonomy code).** | The draft's single transaction and queue enqueue contradict how `Map` is built; this keeps acceptance atomic and the Atlas fresh without waiting for a pass. |
| D-L3-42 | **Lanes and merge rules** (see "Lanes and tasks"): foundation F1 → F2 → F3 land first and alone; each later lane owns a closed file set in its own worktree; shared hot spots have exactly one owner; generated API files are frozen after F2 and regenerated, never hand-merged; every lane PR targets < ~2 500 changed lines excluding generated files; the demotable lanes are E1, NA3, AD's by-facet half and WM2's Nearby (D-L3-35). | Parallel agents with minimal conflicts. |
| D-L3-43 | **Agent loop and CI (C146).** Lanes iterate with `make verify`, focused package tests (`scripts/go-test` for any `-race` run beyond one package) and only the Playwright specs their change affects (`scripts/pw`); whole-repo `make test`, `make test-long`, `make perf` and `make e2e` run once in INT-B/INT-W or in GitHub CI; the `go-fast`/`go-media` tiers of the draft no longer exist. | The shipped dev loop. |
| D-L3-44 | **The Atlas reuses the list-op filter; crate expansion stays in the handler.** `get_song_atlas` embeds `api.SongFilterIn` (with L2's added predicates), uses the same converter as `list_songs`, and the handler expands `crate_id` through `crates.ExpandSongs` before calling `atlas.Get(Query{Filter: songs.ListFilter})`; `atlas` imports `songs` but not `crates`. | One vocabulary for list, Crate and map; no new import edge. |
| D-L3-45 | **No new e2e route.** Specs create their data through the API: draft Songs carry declared tempo and predicted energy; composed Songs get measured values only when the e2e server's Tier A runs on the fake provider's audio, asserted as "present with a basis", never as numbers. Nothing seeds `analyses` rows from the browser. | Per-worker servers and the hermetic rule. |

---

## Contracts (all lanes build against these — do not change without updating this file)

### C133. Packages, dependency order, constants

New packages (all under `internal/`) and additions:

| Package | Role | May import |
|---|---|---|
| `features` | feature derivation (the Reconciler's third step and the recompute hook's entry), the energy prior, Camelot and key parsing, `Verify`, readers | `store`, `sounds` (`CompareTempo`, `TempoBand`, `SubjectOf`), `engine/yue2` (`StyleTerms`), `textfold`, `analysis` (the `*Def` constants only; `analysis` imports no internal package) |
| `sequencer` | pure: integer cost model, exact and heuristic search, explanation | stdlib only |
| `sequence` | `LoadFeatures` (SQL), the `suggest_sequence` service, title injection for explanations | `store`, `sequencer` |
| `nextactions` | the rule registry and engine; **the one new Muse-text reader** | `store`, `auth`, `muses` (`Refs` and `LiveIDs` only), `songview`, `sounds`, `songs` |
| `atlas` | axes registry, fixed-point layout, query, neighbours, adherence | `store`, `songs` (`ListFilter`, `FilterSQL`), `sequencer` (cost functions), `textfold` |
| `sounds` (existing) | gains `subject.go` (`SubjectOf`), the `Map` split, `SetRecomputeHook`, `impact.go`, `proposals*.go` | as L1 |
| `records` (existing, L2) | gains `sequence.go` (the `sequence` slot op) and `ProposeWith` | as L2 C118, plus `sequence`, `sequencer` |
| `recordview` (existing, L2) | gains `sequence.go` (`include=sequence`) | as L2 |
| `analysis`, `analysis/ffmpeg`, `ears` (existing) | gain `key.go`, `energy.go`, `ffmpeg.Mono` and the analyzers | as M2b |
| `provenance` (existing) | gains `FeatureStep`, `FeatureChanges`, `Options.Features`, `Report.FeaturesComputed` | as L1 (no import of `features`) |
| `trackctx` (existing) | gains `nextactions.go` (a nil-safe reader interface and the C139.4 assembly) | as L1/L2 |
| `songview` (existing) | gains `features.go` (`FeaturesBlock`, by SQL over `song_features`) | as L1 |
| `muses` (existing) | gains `Service.LiveIDs(ctx) ([]string, error)`: ids of non-hidden Muses, one statement, no text (`live_ids.go`, NA1) | as L1 |

`api` consumes them through `Deps` fields (`Features *features.Service`, `Sequence *sequence.Service`, `NextActions *nextactions.Service`, `Atlas *atlas.Service`; nil ⇒ 503 `UNAVAILABLE {component}`, the M2a/M3/L1/L2 convention; taxonomy proposals live on the existing `Sounds`
dependency; the Record ops use L2's `Records`/`RecordView`). `app.Build` constructs and wires everything (`app/l3.go`), adapts `features.Service` into `provenance.FeatureStep` (mapping its result to `provenance.FeatureChanges`), `nextactions.Service` into `trackctx.NextActionReader`,
`features.Service.ComputeTx` into `sounds.Service.SetRecomputeHook`, and starts nothing new: features are drained by the L1 `Run` loop (C95).

Import-graph rules (`TestImportGraph_L3`, a **new** file extending the L1/L2 rules; the L1/L2 test files are not edited except the one `allowed`-table line L2 owns):
- the packages above import only what the table lists; `sequencer` imports no `internal/` package;
- `provenance` and `picks` do **not** import `features` (the step is wired through an interface in `app`, the `nopPicks` pattern, and L1's direct-import table for `provenance` stays unchanged); `records` imports `sequence` and `sequencer` but not `features`;
- `export`, `exports`, `notify`, `audit`, `backup`, `importer`, `jobs`, `generation`, `lineage`, `library`, `redact`, every `subsonic*` package and the L2 export-bound packages `tape`, `albums`, `liner`, `cover`, `curation`, `records` **must not reach `nextactions`** (it reads Muse text);
- **Muse-text readers.** L1/L2's readers are `muses`, `board`, `songview`, `recordview`; L3 adds exactly one, `nextactions`. Because `atlas`, `features`, `sequence` reach `muses` transitively through `songs`, the guard is an **AST scan** (new, `TestMuseReaders_L3`; nothing equivalent exists today): it lists
  every call site of `muses.Service.Get/Ref/Refs/List/LiveIDs` in non-test Go and fails on a caller outside {`muses`, `board`, `songview`, `recordview`, `nextactions`, `songs` (its existing reads), and the composition roots `api` and `app`}. `atlas`, `features`, `sequence` never call `muses` and never mention the muses tables in SQL
  (`TestSQL_MuseTables_Allowlist` is unchanged and green). `nextactions` is **not** added to the SQL allowlist: its rules need *live* (non-hidden) Muses, which it obtains through `muses.Service.LiveIDs` and passes into its batch statements as a `json_each(?)` parameter, never by naming `muses` in SQL (`TestNextActions_NoMuseTableSQL` greps its SQL).

`store` gains nothing. `textfold` gains nothing.

Constants (one file per package, `params.go`):
`features.Def = "mm-features/1"`, `features.PriorDef = "mm-energy-prior/1"`, `features.BatchSongs = 100`;
`analysis.KeyDef = "mm-key/1"`, `analysis.EnergyDef = "mm-energy/1"`, `analysis.MonoRate = 11025`, `analysis.MonoMaxS = 240`;
`sequencer.MaxSongs = 60`, `ExactMax = 12` (the test-only override is an unexported `planOpts.exactMax`/`forceHeuristic`, C137.4), `BeamWidth = 8`, `MaxLocalPasses = 50`, `TempoSaturateMicro = 200 000`,
`EnergySaturateMicro = 400 000`, `NeutralMicro = 500 000`, `ShapeNeutralMicro = 250 000`; `sequencer.Strategies` (the registry: `arc`, `ramp_up`, `steady`);
`atlas.DefaultPage = 400`, `MaxPage = 600`, `MaxScope = 5 000`, `QuantNumeric = 128`, `QuantMixed = 64`, `MaxNeighbours = 25`,
`TitleRunes = 60`, `AgentDefaultPage = 100`, `LayoutCacheEntries = 4`, `LayoutCacheTTL = 60 s` (memory bound only), `AdherenceMinN = 3`, `Within5Pct = 5.0`;
`nextactions.MaxItems = 50`, `DefaultItems = 20`, `PerCode = 5`, `ContextMuseMax = 2`, `ContextMuseMinPriority = 50`, `MuseTitleRunes = 60`, `SequencePrecheckSlotsMin = 4`;
`sounds.ProposalMaxOpenPerPrincipal = 10`, `ProposalMaxOpen = 50`, `ImpactMaxSongs = 20 000`, `ProposalMaxMappings = 50`
(`= MaxMappingsPerCall`), `DecisionNoteMax = 300`.

### C134. Migration `internal/store/migrations/0011_agents_atlas.sql` (full DDL) and write rules

```sql
-- 0011_agents_atlas.sql — L3: features, sequences, taxonomy proposals. Writes only queue rows (no feature, proposal or sequence row).

-- ---- Features (derived caches; rebuilt by the Reconciler; deletable, D-L3-3) ---------------------------
CREATE TABLE take_features (
  track_id           TEXT PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES tracks(id),
  bpm_x100           INTEGER CHECK (bpm_x100 IS NULL OR bpm_x100 BETWEEN 2000 AND 30000),            -- POSITION tempo (D-L3-5)
  bpm_basis          TEXT    CHECK (bpm_basis IS NULL OR bpm_basis IN ('measured','declared','plan')),
  bpm_requested_x100 INTEGER CHECK (bpm_requested_x100 IS NULL OR bpm_requested_x100 BETWEEN 2000 AND 30000),   -- declared, else plan
  bpm_measured_x100  INTEGER CHECK (bpm_measured_x100 IS NULL OR bpm_measured_x100 BETWEEN 2000 AND 30000),     -- Ears `tempo`
  bpm_octave         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (bpm_octave IN (0,1)),
  energy_x10000      INTEGER CHECK (energy_x10000 IS NULL OR energy_x10000 BETWEEN 0 AND 10000),
  energy_basis       TEXT    CHECK (energy_basis IS NULL OR energy_basis IN ('measured','predicted')),
  key_num            INTEGER CHECK (key_num IS NULL OR key_num BETWEEN 1 AND 12),
  key_letter         TEXT    CHECK (key_letter IS NULL OR key_letter IN ('A','B')),
  key_basis          TEXT    CHECK (key_basis IS NULL OR key_basis IN ('measured','declared','plan')),
  duration_s         REAL    CHECK (duration_s IS NULL OR duration_s > 0),
  duration_expected_s REAL   CHECK (duration_expected_s IS NULL OR duration_expected_s > 0),         -- scores.bars * seconds_per_bar
  detail_json        TEXT    CHECK (detail_json IS NULL OR json_valid(detail_json)),
  digest             TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(digest) = 64),
  feature_def        TEXT NOT NULL,                                                                  -- 'mm-features/1'
  computed_at        TEXT NOT NULL,
  CHECK ((bpm_x100 IS NULL) = (bpm_basis IS NULL)),
  CHECK ((energy_x10000 IS NULL) = (energy_basis IS NULL)),
  CHECK ((key_num IS NULL) = (key_letter IS NULL) AND (key_num IS NULL) = (key_basis IS NULL))
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE song_features (                                   -- from the Song's SUBJECT exactly as sounds.SubjectOf defines it (C135.5, D-L3-39)
  song_id            TEXT PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES songs(id),
  subject_kind       TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (subject_kind IN ('take','draft')),
  subject_id         TEXT NOT NULL,
  subject_note       TEXT CHECK (subject_note IS NULL OR subject_note IN ('all_takes_discarded')),        -- set when a Song that HAS takes is derived from its draft because every take is discarded (C135.5)
  bpm_x100           INTEGER CHECK (bpm_x100 IS NULL OR bpm_x100 BETWEEN 2000 AND 30000),
  bpm_basis          TEXT    CHECK (bpm_basis IS NULL OR bpm_basis IN ('measured','declared','plan')),
  bpm_requested_x100 INTEGER CHECK (bpm_requested_x100 IS NULL OR bpm_requested_x100 BETWEEN 2000 AND 30000),
  bpm_measured_x100  INTEGER CHECK (bpm_measured_x100 IS NULL OR bpm_measured_x100 BETWEEN 2000 AND 30000),
  bpm_octave         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (bpm_octave IN (0,1)),
  energy_x10000      INTEGER CHECK (energy_x10000 IS NULL OR energy_x10000 BETWEEN 0 AND 10000),
  energy_basis       TEXT    CHECK (energy_basis IS NULL OR energy_basis IN ('measured','predicted')),
  key_num            INTEGER CHECK (key_num IS NULL OR key_num BETWEEN 1 AND 12),
  key_letter         TEXT    CHECK (key_letter IS NULL OR key_letter IN ('A','B')),
  key_basis          TEXT    CHECK (key_basis IS NULL OR key_basis IN ('measured','declared','plan')),
  duration_s         REAL    CHECK (duration_s IS NULL OR duration_s > 0),
  duration_expected_s REAL   CHECK (duration_expected_s IS NULL OR duration_expected_s > 0),
  family             TEXT CHECK (family   IS NULL OR length(family)   BETWEEN 1 AND 40),             -- PRIMARY Sound values (smallest song_sounds.ord, D-L3-9)
  vocal              TEXT CHECK (vocal    IS NULL OR length(vocal)    BETWEEN 1 AND 40),
  era                TEXT CHECK (era      IS NULL OR length(era)      BETWEEN 1 AND 40),
  mood               TEXT CHECK (mood     IS NULL OR length(mood)     BETWEEN 1 AND 40),
  language           TEXT CHECK (language IS NULL OR length(language) BETWEEN 1 AND 40),
  digest             TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(digest) = 64),
  feature_def        TEXT NOT NULL,
  computed_at        TEXT NOT NULL,
  CHECK ((bpm_x100 IS NULL) = (bpm_basis IS NULL)),
  CHECK ((energy_x10000 IS NULL) = (energy_basis IS NULL)),
  CHECK ((key_num IS NULL) = (key_letter IS NULL) AND (key_num IS NULL) = (key_basis IS NULL))
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE INDEX song_features_xy     ON song_features(bpm_x100, energy_x10000);
CREATE INDEX song_features_family ON song_features(family, vocal);
CREATE INDEX song_features_key    ON song_features(key_num, key_letter);

-- ---- Sequences (append-only; the explanation of an applied ordering, D-L3-10/13) --------------------
CREATE TABLE record_sequences (
  id                TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                          -- UUIDv7
  record_id         TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES records(id),
  record_revision   INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (record_revision >= 1),       -- the revision the edit was made against
  strategy          TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(strategy) BETWEEN 1 AND 24),     -- validated in Go (sequencer.Strategies, D-L3-40)
  first_song_id     TEXT REFERENCES songs(id),
  last_song_id      TEXT REFERENCES songs(id),
  order_before_json TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(order_before_json) AND json_type(order_before_json) = 'array'),   -- slot ids
  order_after_json  TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(order_after_json)  AND json_type(order_after_json)  = 'array'),
  exact             INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (exact IN (0,1)),
  cost_input        INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (cost_input  >= 0),                                                       -- J of the input order as given (even when it violates the endpoints), units of 1e-10
  cost_output       INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (cost_output >= 0),
  coverage_json     TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(coverage_json)),
  digest            TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(digest) = 64),
  explanation_json  TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(explanation_json)),                                              -- mm.sequence/1: ids and numbers only
  actor             TEXT NOT NULL,                                                                                   -- Principal.Key()
  actor_kind        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (actor_kind IN ('human','agent')),
  actor_name        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(actor_name) BETWEEN 1 AND 120),
  at                TEXT NOT NULL,
  CHECK (json_array_length(order_before_json) = json_array_length(order_after_json))                               -- NO cost CHECK: with endpoints the output may cost more than the input order (C137.4)
);
CREATE INDEX record_sequences_record ON record_sequences(record_id, at);
CREATE TRIGGER record_sequences_immutable BEFORE UPDATE ON record_sequences
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'record_sequences: append-only'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER record_sequences_no_delete BEFORE DELETE ON record_sequences
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'record_sequences: append-only'); END;

-- ---- Taxonomy proposals (D-L3-20) --------------------------------------------------------------------
CREATE TABLE taxonomy_proposals (
  id             TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                             -- UUIDv7
  state          TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('open','accepted','rejected','expired')),
  base_version   INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (base_version >= 1),   -- the taxonomy version the proposal was validated against
  rationale      TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(rationale) BETWEEN 1 AND 500),
  mappings_json  TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(mappings_json) AND json_type(mappings_json) = 'array'
                                      AND json_array_length(mappings_json) BETWEEN 1 AND 50),   -- [{term, facet, value, op}] folded, validated, in call order
  mappings_digest TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(mappings_digest) = 64),
  impact_json    TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(impact_json)),   -- snapshot at proposal time (C140.3)
  principal_id   TEXT NOT NULL,                                -- Principal.Key()
  principal_kind TEXT NOT NULL,
  principal_name TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(principal_name) BETWEEN 1 AND 120),
  created_at     TEXT NOT NULL,
  decided_at     TEXT,
  decided_by     TEXT,                                         -- Principal.Key() of the human; 'system:sounds' when expired
  decided_note   TEXT CHECK (decided_note IS NULL OR length(decided_note) BETWEEN 1 AND 300),
  accepted_json  TEXT CHECK (accepted_json IS NULL OR (json_valid(accepted_json) AND json_type(accepted_json) = 'array')),   -- accepted mapping indices
  skipped_json   TEXT CHECK (skipped_json IS NULL OR (json_valid(skipped_json) AND json_type(skipped_json) = 'array')),     -- [{index, reason}]
  applied_version INTEGER CHECK (applied_version IS NULL OR applied_version >= 1),
  CHECK ((state = 'open') = (decided_at IS NULL)),
  CHECK ((decided_at IS NULL) = (decided_by IS NULL)),
  CHECK ((state = 'accepted') = (applied_version IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK ((state = 'accepted') = (accepted_json IS NOT NULL))
);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX taxonomy_proposals_open_dup ON taxonomy_proposals(principal_id, mappings_digest) WHERE state = 'open';
CREATE INDEX taxonomy_proposals_state ON taxonomy_proposals(state, created_at);
CREATE TRIGGER taxonomy_proposals_no_delete BEFORE DELETE ON taxonomy_proposals
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'taxonomy_proposals: no physical delete'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER taxonomy_proposals_frozen BEFORE UPDATE OF id, base_version, rationale, mappings_json, mappings_digest, impact_json,
  principal_id, principal_kind, principal_name, created_at ON taxonomy_proposals
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'taxonomy_proposals: frozen column'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER taxonomy_proposals_decided BEFORE UPDATE ON taxonomy_proposals WHEN OLD.state <> 'open'
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'taxonomy_proposals: decided proposals are immutable'); END;

-- ---- L1 amendments (D-L3-9) --------------------------------------------------------------------------
ALTER TABLE song_sounds ADD COLUMN ord INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (ord >= 0);   -- derived cache: the position of the row in the emitted rows, written by the Sounds compute (songDigest compute_def mm-sound-compute/2, D-L3-9)

-- A new Ears measurement re-derives the features of its take (L1's pd_analyses_tempo already covers `tempo`).
CREATE TRIGGER pd_analyses_feat AFTER INSERT ON analyses WHEN NEW.analyzer IN ('key','energy') BEGIN
  INSERT INTO provenance_dirty(kind, id, at) VALUES ('track', NEW.track_id, strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%fZ','now'))
  ON CONFLICT(kind, id) DO UPDATE SET at = excluded.at, tries = 0;
END;

-- Backfill = the steady-state path (D-L1-4): every take and every draft is re-marked once, so the Reconciler recomputes the
-- Sound subjects (new compute definition) and derives the features.
INSERT INTO provenance_dirty(kind, id, at)
  SELECT 'track', id, strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%fZ','now') FROM tracks WHERE 1
  ON CONFLICT(kind, id) DO UPDATE SET at = excluded.at, tries = 0;
INSERT INTO provenance_dirty(kind, id, at)
  SELECT 'draft', id, strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%fZ','now') FROM recipe_drafts WHERE 1
  ON CONFLICT(kind, id) DO UPDATE SET at = excluded.at, tries = 0;
```

**C134.1 Notes on the DDL.** (1) 0011 creates no feature, sequence or proposal row: ids, digests and values are Go-computed; the queue re-seed and the emptiness of the four new tables are the only data effects. (2) `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` is empty after 0011 on a database upgraded from schemas 5–10
(`record_sequences.record_id` references `records`, which exists since 0010; the order is fixed by the migration numbers). (3) `take_features`/`song_features` are **caches** (no triggers): `features` replaces rows by delete-and-insert inside the Reconciler's phase-B transaction or inside a `sounds` recompute
transaction (D-L1-24 pattern, D-L3-41); they are added to the `TestNoDeleteSQL` allowlist (`internal/store/nodelete_test.go`), the only allowlist change. (4) `record_sequences.cost_*` are the integer `J` of C137.3 (units 10⁻¹⁰); there is **no** `cost_output <= cost_input` CHECK: without endpoints the planner
returns an order no worse than the input (verified by `Verify` F4 and `TestPlan_NeverWorse`), but with `first_song_id`/`last_song_id` the input order is usually infeasible and the output may cost more; `cost_input` is always the cost of the input order as given. (5) `taxonomy_proposals.state` has no `superseded`
(an identical open set is *returned*, not superseded); `expired` is written only by the decide path when every mapping went stale (`decided_by = 'system:sounds'`). (6) `song_sounds.ord` is a column on a derived cache: the L1 `TestSchema_*` column lists are amended by F1 and `insertSounds` writes it
(`ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN` on a WITHOUT ROWID table is asserted by `TestMigration0011_Applies`); existing rows keep `ord = 0` until the Reconciler recomputes each Song once (the re-seed below). (7) The `WHERE 1` guards keep SQLite from parsing `ON CONFLICT` as a join ambiguity in `INSERT … SELECT`.
(8) Timestamps are written with `store.FormatTime` (24 characters, D-L1-25). `features` orders no takes itself: the subject comes from `sounds.SubjectOf`, which keeps L1's `recorded_at DESC, id DESC` (a 20- and a 24-character stamp inside one second may order either way — identically for Sounds and features, because they share the function). (9) **No L2 object changes**: `record_changes`, `record_slots` and `records` keep their L2 rev 3 DDL (the `sequence` op writes only `slot_moved` and `proposed`
rows with a `detail_json`); no object of 0008–0010 other than `song_sounds` is altered.

**C134.2 Write rules** (`TestSQL_WriteOwners_L3`, a new file in `internal/archtest/`, greps non-test Go for `INSERT INTO|UPDATE|DELETE FROM <table>` outside the owners):

| Table / column | Owner (writes) | Other writers |
|---|---|---|
| `take_features`, `song_features` | `features` (`ComputeTx`, `SweepStaleTx`: called by the Reconciler's phase B / sweep, and by the `sounds` recompute hook through `app`) | none |
| `record_sequences` | `records` (`sequence.go`, inside `EditSlots`/`ProposeWith`) | none |
