| `record_changes` rows with `detail_json.by = "sequencer"` or `detail_json.sequence_id` | `records.Service` (same transaction as the `record_sequences` row) | none |
| `taxonomy_proposals` | `sounds` (`proposals*.go`) | none |
| `sound_taxonomy_versions`, `sound_terms` (from an accepted proposal) | `sounds` (`applyTx`, called by `Map` and by `Decide`) | none |
| `song_sounds.ord` | `sounds` (compute) | none |
| `provenance_dirty` | the L1 triggers, `pd_analyses_feat` and the migration's re-seed | no L3 Go code writes it |

### C135. Features (`internal/features`)

```go
type Basis string // "measured" | "declared" | "plan" | "predicted"
type Camelot struct{ Num int; Letter string }                  // Num 1..12; Letter "A" = minor, "B" = major
func CamelotOf(tonicPC int, mode string) Camelot                // tonicPC 0..11 (C=0); mode "major" | "minor"
func (c Camelot) String() string                                // "8A"
func KeyName(tonicPC int, mode string) string                   // "A minor", "F# minor", "Eb major" (committed spelling table, C135.1)
func ParseStyleKey(style string) (tonicPC int, mode string, ok bool)   // C135.3
func ParseABCKey(abc string) (tonicPC int, mode string, ok bool)       // the K: header, C135.3
func PredictEnergy(f Facets) (x10000 int, ok bool)                     // C135.4
type Facets struct{ Mood, GenreFamily, TempoBand []string }

type Row struct {                                               // one take_features / song_features payload
	BPMx100 *int; BPMBasis Basis; BPMRequestedx100, BPMMeasuredx100 *int; BPMOctave bool
	Energyx10000 *int; EnergyBasis Basis
	Key *Camelot; KeyBasis Basis
	DurationS, DurationExpectedS *float64; Detail json.RawMessage
}
type TakeInput struct {                                         // everything DeriveTake reads (C135.2)
	DeclaredBPM *float64; StyleBPM *int; PlanBPM *float64; MeasuredTempo *TempoMeasure
	MeasuredKey *KeyMeasure; MeasuredEnergy *EnergyMeasure
	StyleKey, PlanKey *KeyGuess; Facets Facets; DurationS float64; ExpectedDurationS *float64
	RecipeKnown bool
}
func DeriveTake(in TakeInput) Row                                // pure, deterministic

// the Song subject is sounds.Subject{Kind take/draft/none, ID} from sounds.SubjectOf (D-L3-39); features defines no second rule
func New(st *store.Store, now func() time.Time) *Service
func (s *Service) ComputeTx(ctx context.Context, tx *sql.Tx, env Env, trackIDs, songIDs []string) (Changes, error)   // Reconciler phase B
func (s *Service) SweepStaleTx(ctx context.Context, tx *sql.Tx, env Env, limit int) (n int, err error)
func (s *Service) Verify(ctx context.Context) ([]Anomaly, error)                                                     // C135.6
func (s *Service) Take(ctx context.Context, trackID string) (Row, bool, error)
func (s *Service) Songs(ctx context.Context, songIDs []string) (map[string]SongRow, error)                           // one statement
func (s *Service) Takes(ctx context.Context, trackIDs []string) (map[string]Row, error)                              // one statement
```
`Env = store.Env` (L1). `provenance.FeatureStep` is `interface{ ComputeTx(ctx, tx, env, trackIDs, songIDs []string) (FeatureChanges, error); SweepStaleTx(ctx, tx, env, limit int) (int, error) }` with the provenance-local `FeatureChanges{Takes, Songs int}`, frozen by F2; `app` adapts `*features.Service` (whose own `ComputeTx` returns `features.Changes`) to it.

**C135.1 Camelot table (binding; `TestCamelot_Table`).** Major keys: C 8B · G 9B · D 10B · A 11B · E 12B · B 1B · F♯ 2B · D♭ 3B · A♭ 4B · E♭ 5B ·
B♭ 6B · F 7B. Minor keys: A 8A · E 9A · B 10A · F♯ 11A · C♯ 12A · G♯ 1A · D♯ 2A · B♭ 3A · F 4A · C 5A · G 6A · D 7A. A relative pair shares its number
(`CamelotOf(9,"minor")` = 8A, `CamelotOf(0,"major")` = 8B). `KeyName` uses one **committed spelling table** (`features/keynames.go`) indexed by pitch class: major `C Db D Eb E F F# G Ab A Bb B`; minor `C C# D Eb E F F# G G# A Bb B`. The names are therefore `A minor`, `Bb minor` (pitch class 10), `F# minor`, `Eb major`, `C major`, `F# major`; `TestKeyName_Roundtrip` asserts `ParseStyleKey(KeyName(pc, mode)) = (pc, mode)` for all 24 keys.

**C135.2 `DeriveTake`** (pure; the unit of every table test):
- **Tempo.** `requested` = `DeclaredBPM` (the `tracks.bpm_declared` value) else `StyleBPM` (the `yue2.StyleTerms` BPM) else `PlanBPM`
  (basis `declared`, `declared`, `plan` respectively); `measured` = `MeasuredTempo.BPM` when non-nil. Fixed point `round(x·100)` (half away
  from zero); values outside 20.00–300.00 BPM are discarded (`detail.discarded.bpm`). **Position:** if `measured` and `requested` exist and the L1
  comparison (`sounds.CompareTempo(requested, measured *float64) (mismatch, octave bool)`; `tracks.bpm_declared` is an `INTEGER`, read as an integer and converted) reports `octave` ⇒ position = `requested` (its basis), `BPMOctave = true`; else `measured` (basis
  `measured`) when present; else `requested`. Both values are always stored in their own columns.
- **Key.** `MeasuredKey` with a non-nil key ⇒ basis `measured`; else `StyleKey` ⇒ `declared`; else `PlanKey` ⇒ `plan`; else NULL. A disagreement between a
  measured and a declared/plan key is recorded in `detail.key {measured, declared|plan, relation: "same"|"relative"|"other"}` and the measured key wins.
- **Energy.** `MeasuredEnergy.Energy` non-nil ⇒ `round(e·10000)`, basis `measured`; else `PredictEnergy(Facets)` ⇒ basis `predicted`; else NULL.
  A reference take (`RecipeKnown = false`) has empty `Facets`, so it has energy only when measured (D-L3-4).
- `DurationS`, `ExpectedDurationS` pass through (`> 0` else NULL). `Detail` holds `{octave, discarded, key, tempo: {confidence, octave_ambiguous}}` (numbers and codes only).
- **Which analysis row counts.** `analyses` rows are immutable and keyed `(track, analyzer, version)`. For each of `tempo`, `key`, `energy`, `DeriveTake` reads only rows whose version string starts with the **current definition constant** (`mm-tempo/1 `, `mm-key/1 `, `mm-energy/1 `, i.e. the text before the first space equals `analysis.KeyDef` etc.); when several exist (different tool suffix) the greatest `version` string wins. A row of another definition (for example a future `mm-key/2`) is ignored until the code constant changes; its insert still fires `pd_analyses_feat`, but the digest is unchanged so nothing is rewritten. The energy loudness term is **frozen at analysis time** (it is inside `metrics_json.lufs`): a later `loudness` row does not re-derive energy (no trigger on `loudness`); `metamusic analyze --force` re-runs `energy` and the new row re-derives the take. `TestDeriveTake_AnalysisRowSelection`.
- **Digest** = `sha256(canonical JSON {def, requested bpm basis inputs, tempo analysis version + sha256(metrics_json), key analysis version + sha256, energy analysis version + sha256, facets digest = the take's sound_state digest, declared/plan/style key inputs, duration, expected duration})`.

**C135.3 Key parsing.**
- `ParseStyleKey(style)` considers each comma-separated **term** (trimmed) of the style in order and returns the first match of two forms. (a) **`in <key>`**: the term contains `in ` (case-insensitive) followed by a tonic letter in **either case**, an optional accidental (`#`, `♯`, `b`, `♭`) and a mode word (`major|maj|minor|min`, case-insensitive): `in a minor`, `in A Minor`, `in F# major` ⇒ ok. (b) **Whole-term key**: the term *starts with* an **upper-case** tonic letter, an optional accidental and a mode word (`F# major anthem`, `Bb minor`, `A minor`), or the whole term is exactly a tonic plus `m` (`Am`, `F#m`, `Bbm`; the bare `m` form is never accepted inside a longer term). A lower-case tonic outside `in ` is never a key (`a major hit` ⇒ not ok), and a mode word without a tonic never is (`major label vibes`). The tonic letter is upper-cased and `♯`/`♭` normalised to `#`/`b`. It never reads lyrics. Table `TestParseStyleKey_Table` (18 rows: `"reggae, in A minor, 76bpm"` ⇒ 9 minor; `"in a minor"` ⇒ 9 minor; `"F# major anthem"` ⇒ 6 major; `"Bb minor"` ⇒ 10 minor; `"a major hit"` ⇒ **not ok**; `"warm, Am"` ⇒ 9 minor; `"drama"` ⇒ not ok; `"major label vibes"` ⇒ not ok; `"jam in e minor groove"` ⇒ 4 minor; `"Amazing"` ⇒ not ok).
- `ParseABCKey(abc)` reads the first `K:` header line: `K:C`, `K:Am`, `K:F#m`, `K:Bb`, `K:D mix`, `K:G dor`; modes map to the nearest major/minor (`maj|ion|lyd|mix` ⇒ major with the written tonic; `min|aeo|dor|phr|loc` ⇒ minor); `K:none`/missing ⇒ not ok. `TestParseABCKey_Table` (14 rows incl. the M1 fixture archive's `K:C`).
- Inputs come only from DB text: the draft's or recipe's `request_json` (`style`, inline `abc`). **No file is read** by the Reconciler.

**C135.4 Predicted energy (`mm-energy-prior/1`, committed in `features/prior.go`, pinned by `testdata/prior_v1.golden.json`).** Values × 10 000:
- `mood`: upbeat 7500 · chill 2500 · melancholic 3000 · romantic 3500 · dark 5500 · dreamy 2500 · energetic 9000 · nostalgic 4000 · aggressive 9500 · warm 3500.
- `genre_family`: ambient 1500 · classical 3000 · folk 3500 · singer-songwriter 3000 · jazz 4000 · blues 4000 · country 4500 · reggae 5000 · world 5000 · cinematic 5000 · r-and-b 5000 · funk-soul 6500 · pop 6000 · latin 6500 · electronic 7000 · hip-hop 7000 · rock 7500 · dance 8500 · punk 9000 · metal 9500.
- `tempo_band`: slow 2500 · mid 4500 · upbeat 6500 · fast 8000 · rapid 9000.
`PredictEnergy(f)`: for each of the three facets that has ≥ 1 value, take the **mean** of its values' table entries (integer division rounded half up); the result is the mean of the facet means (half up); no facet ⇒ `ok = false`. Examples pinned by `TestPredictEnergy_Table`: `chill + reggae + slow` ⇒ (2500 + 5000 + 2500)/3 = **3333**; `energetic + dance + fast` ⇒ **8500**; only `reggae` ⇒ **5000**; `pop + electronic` (one facet, two values) ⇒ mean(6000, 7000) = 6500; none ⇒ not ok; an unknown value is skipped (a facet whose values are all unknown counts as absent). Adding a facet value to the taxonomy without a prior row is a `TestFeatures_RegistryGolden` failure (extension move 1).

**C135.5 Reconciler step and the recompute hook (C95 amendment).** `FeatureStep.ComputeTx` runs **after** the Sounds compute in phase B, for the dirty takes (their `take_features`) and for the *affected Songs* of that pass (their `song_features`); the same `features.ComputeTx(ctx, tx, env, nil, songIDs)` is called by the `sounds` recompute hook (D-L3-41) after a taxonomy change. **Subject rule** (D-L3-39): `sounds.SubjectOf` — the Song's Pick take (`song_picks.take_id`), else its newest non-discarded take (`recorded_at DESC, id DESC`), else its fulfilled draft (`subject_kind = 'draft'`; the row is derived from the draft's `request_json` style/ABC and the Song's `song_sounds`, bases `declared`/`plan`/`predicted` only), else none (no row). A Song that has takes but fell through to its draft because every take is discarded gets `subject_note = 'all_takes_discarded'` (the human threw the renders away, so the Song is "requested" again, matching L1's `draft` state). The Atlas tooltip and `get_song.features.subject` carry the note. A Song's row copies the subject's feature columns and adds the five primary Sound values (C93 + D-L3-9). **Song digest** = `sha256(canonical JSON {def, subject_kind, subject_id, subject_note, for a take subject: that take's `take_features.digest`; for a draft subject: sha256 of the draft's style text, inline ABC and declared-BPM inputs; the Song's `sound_state` digest; the five primary Sound values})`. Rows whose digest is unchanged are not rewritten (`TestFeatures_DigestUnchangedNoRewrite`, `TestFeatures_SongDigest`). The step is idempotent and never writes outside its two tables. `SweepStaleTx` rewrites rows whose `feature_def`, digest or subject changed (startup and hourly sweeps), in batches of `BatchSongs`. **Taxonomy changes do not rely on it**: `sounds.Service` calls the recompute hook inside each recompute transaction with the Song ids it rewrote, so `song_features.family/vocal/era/mood/language` change atomically with `song_sounds` (`TestMap_RefreshesFeaturesInPlace`).

**C135.6 `Verify` (L3 invariants F1–F6; `library verify` runs them after L1 and L2; `Anomaly{Code, Subject, Detail}`).**
- **F1** (after the queue is empty) every live Song with a subject has exactly one `song_features` row whose `subject` equals `sounds.SubjectOf` (including `subject_note` for a Song whose takes are all discarded and that fell through to its draft); none for retired Songs or Songs whose subject is none.
- **F2** every track has a `take_features` row; no row has basis `measured` without the matching `analyses` row; `bpm_octave = 1` only when both tempo values exist.
- **F3** the `song_features` copy columns equal the subject's `take_features` row (draft subjects excepted).
- **F4** every `record_sequences.order_after_json` is a permutation of `order_before_json`; every `record_changes` row with `detail.sequence_id` finds its `record_sequences` row; `cost_output ≤ cost_input` **only for rows with `first_song_id IS NULL AND last_song_id IS NULL`** (endpoint rows may cost more).
- **F5** `taxonomy_proposals`: an `accepted` row's `applied_version` exists in `sound_taxonomy_versions` with note `proposal <id>`; `open` rows are at most `ProposalMaxOpen`; every `open` row's mappings are well-formed (re-validated against the index *now* only as a warning `proposal_stale`).
- **F6** every `song_sounds` row set of a Song with > 1 value in one facet has distinct `ord` values, and no `sound_state` row is stale against the `mm-sound-compute/2` digest once the queue is empty (L1's I8 freshness check, extended).
### C136. Ears: the `key` and `energy` analyzers and the shared decode (`internal/analysis`, `internal/analysis/ffmpeg`, `internal/ears`)

**C136.0 Shared decode (amends L1 C94, D-L3-8).** `ffmpeg.Mono(ctx, workPath, maxS) (analysis.PCM, error)` decodes `-ac 1 -ar 11025 -t 240 -f f32le -` (the argv of C94, unchanged: `TestFFmpeg_MonoDecodeArgv` next to L1's `argv_tempo_test.go`), `analysis.PCM{Rate int; Samples []float32; AnalyzedS float64}`; NaN and Inf samples are zeroed as L1 does. `ears.TierA` gains `Mono(ctx, path, maxS)` and **drops `Tempo` from the interface**; `(*FFmpeg).Tempo(ctx, workPath, durationS)` stays as a wrapper (`Mono` + `analysis.ComputeTempo`, the short-file shortcut kept) so L1's tempo tests keep their expectations. The pure functions are `analysis.ComputeTempo(x []float32, rate int) Tempo` (the C94 algorithm, **unchanged name and place**), `ComputeKey(PCM) Key`, `ComputeEnergy(PCM, lufs *float64) Energy`. `ears.AnalyzeTrack` decodes **once** when any of the three analyzers is absent (or `Force`), runs each missing one, stores `analyses` rows (tier `A`, versions `mm-tempo/1 <tool>`, `mm-key/1 <tool>`, `mm-energy/1 <tool>`) and treats a failure as a warning in `Outcome.Failed` (never failing the take). `earstest.TierA` (the single file `earstest.go`) implements `Mono` as a deterministic function of the path (a seeded buffer) and the three estimators run on it, so fakes exercise the real functions. `TestEars_OneDecodeForFeatures` (the fake counts `Mono` calls: 1 when all three are missing, 1 when only `key` is missing, 0 when all exist). `analysis.BlindSpotFacts` gains `Key` and `Energy` (conditional blind spots in the fixed order after `Tempo`).

**C136.1 Key (`mm-key/1`, `analysis/key.go`, pure Go, deterministic).** **Step 0 (E1, before anything is frozen): prototype and measure.** The window, the `r ≥ 0.45` floor, the `0.04` margin and the "≥ 22 of 24" test bar below are *starting values*, not contract: E1 first runs the prototype over `FixtureL3Audio` (24 keys, plain and −35 cents) and the reference-recording set, commits the measured numbers in the constants' comments, and only then pins the thresholds and test bars (a bar is never lowered silently: lowering it is an entry in the private notes). If the prototype cannot reach a bar worth shipping, `analysis.KeyEnabled = false` (D-L3-35) and key stays declared/plan/null. Input: the PCM (≤ 240 s, < 20 s ⇒ no key). (1) STFT: Hann window **8192**, hop **4096** at 11 025 Hz (1.35 Hz/bin; a semitone at 110 Hz is 6.5 Hz, about 5 bins; the 4096 window of the first draft gave 2.7 Hz/bin and unreliable nearest-bin voting below ~200 Hz). (2) Magnitude compression
`sqrt(|X|)`; bins 110–2 000 Hz only (A2–B6); each spectral peak's frequency is refined by **parabolic interpolation** on the log-magnitude of its three bins. (3) **Tuning:** accumulate a 100-bin cent histogram of the fractional part of `12·log2(f/440)` weighted by magnitude over spectral peaks;
the circular mean (±50 cents) is the tuning offset, applied before chroma. (4) **Chroma:** each peak (at its interpolated frequency) votes its magnitude into the pitch class of `round(12·log2(f/440) − tuning) mod 12`; summed over time with
per-frame L2 normalisation. (5) Pearson correlation of the 12-vector with each of the 24 rotations of the Krumhansl–Schmuckler major/minor profiles (the published
values: major 6.35 2.23 3.48 2.33 4.38 4.09 2.52 5.19 2.39 3.66 2.29 2.88; minor 6.33 2.68 3.52 5.38 2.60 3.53 2.54 4.75 3.98 2.69 3.34 3.17). (6) `Key = argmax r`; `margin = r₁ − r₂`;
the key is **set** iff `r₁ ≥ 0.45 ∧ margin ≥ 0.04`, else null; `ambiguous_relative` when the runner-up is the relative key with `margin < 0.05`. Stored `metrics_json = {key: "A minor"|null, tonic, mode, camelot, r, margin,
ambiguous_relative, tuning_cents, alternatives: [≤ 3 {key, r}], analyzed_s}` (tonic/mode/camelot null when the key is null). Blind spots added to the dossier: *modulations, modal or non-Western music, atonal or
percussion-dominated passages, a drone that is not the tonic*.
Tests: `TestKey_SyntheticTriads` (24 keys: a I–IV–V–I progression of harmonic-rich triads (6 partials, 1/n amplitudes), 20 s per chord cycle repeated to 60 s, A4 = 440 Hz: **≥ 22 of 24 exactly** and every result within
{exact, relative, fifth} — the same 24 at **−35 cents** detune: ≥ 20 exact), `TestKey_NoiseNil` (white noise and a detuned cluster ⇒ null), `TestKey_Percussive` (a click track ⇒ null), `TestKey_ShortFile` (< 20 s ⇒ null, no error), `TestKey_Deterministic`, `TestKey_Golden`
(pinned JSON of one fixed signal), `TestPerf_Key` (240 s < 3 s CPU, env-scaled).

**C136.2 Energy (`mm-energy/1`, `analysis/energy.go`).** Input: the PCM and the stored `loudness.integrated_lufs` (nil when absent). Components: (a) **onset rate**: the onset-strength envelope of C94 (the unexported `onsetEnvelope` of `tempo.go`, exported as `OnsetEnvelope` by E1 with `ComputeTempo`'s output unchanged), peaks above
`mean + 1.0·σ` at least 0.12 s apart, count ÷ analysed seconds; (b) **centroid**: mean spectral centroid in Hz over STFT frames (1024 Hann, hop 512, 40 Hz–5 kHz) whose energy is within 50 dB of the loudest frame; (c) **loudness** = the stored integrated LUFS.
Each is mapped piecewise-linearly and clamped to [0, 1]: onset 0.5 ⇒ 0 … 5.0 ⇒ 1 (onsets/s); centroid 600 ⇒ 0 … 2 800 ⇒ 1 (Hz); loudness −26 ⇒ 0 … −9 ⇒ 1 (LUFS). `energy = (0.45·m_o + 0.30·m_c + 0.25·m_l) / (sum of the weights present)`. Output `metrics_json = {energy: 0.5321|null, onset_rate, centroid_hz, lufs|null, components: {onset, centroid, loudness|null}, analyzed_s}`;
`energy` is null when `analyzed_s < 20` or the signal is silent (every frame below −60 dBFS). The constants are committed in one table (`energy_params.go`) and the **definition is a decision**, changed only by a new `mm-energy/2` with a golden. Blind spots: *a quiet but dense mix and a loud sparse one are conflated by mastering; genre bias (ambient mastered loud); not perceptual arousal*.
Tests: `TestEnergy_Ordering` (a dense bright click-and-noise track > a slow sine pad > near-silence, monotone, each pairwise gap ≥ 0.15), `TestEnergy_LoudnessTermDropped` (nil LUFS ⇒ weights renormalised; value equals the two-term mean), `TestEnergy_SilentNil`, `TestEnergy_ShortFile`, `TestEnergy_Deterministic`, `TestEnergy_Golden`, `TestEnergy_MappingTable` (the piecewise map at its anchors: 0.5⇒0, 2.75⇒0.5, 5.0⇒1, 600⇒0, 1700⇒0.5, −17.5⇒0.5), `TestPerf_Energy` (240 s < 1 s CPU).

**C136.3 Surfaces.** `ears` dossier and `/context` `perception` gain `key` (`{name, camelot, confidence, ambiguous_relative}` | null) and `energy` (`{value, onset_rate, centroid_hz}` | null), after `tempo_bpm`/`tempo_confidence` (L1). The dossier, perception and `/context` goldens are re-baselined with a reviewed diff. `metamusic analyze` runs the new analyzers by the existing Backfill rule (skip when present, `Force` off); `ears.BackfillReport.Ran` gains the two names.
### C137. The sequencer (`internal/sequencer`, pure; D-L3-11/12/13)

```go
type Camelot struct{ Num int; Letter byte }                         // Num 1..12, Letter 'A' | 'B'
type Song struct {
	ID string
	BPMx100 int                      // 0 = unknown
	Energyx10000 int                 // -1 = unknown (0..10000 otherwise)
	Key *Camelot                     // nil = unknown
	Bases Bases                      // {BPM, Energy, Key string}: carried into the explanation only
}
type Strategy string                 // "arc" | "ramp_up" | "steady"
type Options struct{ Strategy Strategy; First, Last string }          // song ids; either may be empty
type Parts struct{ Tempo, Key, Energy int64 }                         // micro-costs of one transition
type Step struct {
	Position int; SongID string; EnergyRel, Target int                  // 0..10000; Target -1 when the strategy has no shape
	From *Transition; Reasons []Reason; Missing []string
}
type Transition struct{ Cost int64; Parts Parts; TempoDeltaPctX100 *int; KeyRelation string; EnergyDelta *int }
type Result struct {
	Order []string; Exact bool; CostInput, CostOutput int64            // J, units of 1e-10 (C137.3)
	Steps []Step; Coverage Coverage; Warnings []Warning; Digest string
}
func Plan(songs []Song, o Options) (Result, error)                    // errors: ErrTooMany (> MaxSongs), ErrUnknownStrategy, ErrEndpointMissing, ErrEndpointsConflict
func TransitionMicro(a, b Song) Parts                                  // C137.2, used by Distance and the explanation
func Distance(a, b Song) int64                                         // steady weights, absolute (not set-relative) energy; the Atlas neighbours
func ExplainText(s Step, strategy Strategy) string                     // C137.5, deterministic, no titles
func Digest(songs []Song, o Options) string                            // sha256 of the canonical inputs
```

**C137.1 Inputs and relative energy.** `n = len(songs)` (1 ≤ n ≤ 60; n = 1 returns the song unchanged with a `single_song` warning). Energy is rescaled to the set: over the Songs with known energy,
`lo = min`, `hi = max`; `rel = (e − lo)·10000 / hi−lo` (integer division) when `hi − lo ≥ 500`, otherwise every known energy is `5000`. Unknown stays unknown. **Coverage** of a feature = `⌊100·known / n⌋` (percent, integer); the three
coverages are `covT`, `covK`, `covE`.

**C137.2 Costs (binding; integers; micro-units, `M = 1 000 000`).**
- `tempo(a, b)`: either BPM unknown ⇒ `500 000`. With `lo = min`, `hi = max` (centi-BPM): `dd = (hi − lo)·M / lo`; `direct = min(dd·M / 200 000, M)`; `r = hi·M / lo`; `do = |r − 2M| / 2`; `oct = 300 000 + min(do·700 000 / 200 000, 700 000)`; **cost = min(direct, oct)**. (Symmetric. 0 at equal tempo, 1.0 at ≥ 20 % apart, 0.30 at an exact octave.)
- `key(a, b)`: either unknown ⇒ `500 000`. `dn = min(|n₁ − n₂|, 12 − |n₁ − n₂|)`. Same letter: `dn` 0 ⇒ 0, 1 ⇒ 150 000, 2 ⇒ 450 000, 3 ⇒ 700 000, ≥ 4 ⇒ M. Different letter: `dn` 0 ⇒ 100 000 (relative major/minor), 1 ⇒ 500 000, ≥ 2 ⇒ M.
- `energy(a, b)` (on relative energies): either unknown ⇒ `500 000`; else `min(|b − a|·250, M)` (a step of 0.40 or more costs M).
- `shape(e, t)` for the Song at position `p` with relative energy `e` and target `t`: `e` unknown ⇒ `250 000`; else `min((e − t)²·M / 25 000 000, M)`.
- `target(strategy, p, n)`: position fraction `f = p·10000 / (n − 1)` (n = 1 ⇒ 5000). `arc`: `f ≤ 7000 ⇒ 3000 + f`, else `10000 − (f − 7000)·5000 / 3000` (rises 0.3 → 1.0 at 70 %, lands at 0.5). `ramp_up`: `f`. `steady`: no shape.

**C137.3 Objective and weights.** Weights (per hundred): `arc {tempo 100, key 80, energy 50, shape 150}`, `ramp_up {100, 80, 30, 150}`, `steady {100, 80, 80, 0}`. Effective weights multiply by the coverage percent
(`wT·covT`, `wK·covK`, `wE·covE`, `wS·covE`); a feature known for no Song therefore weighs 0 and the sequencer degrades to what is known. The objective of an order is
`J = Σ_{i<n−1} (wT·covT·tempo + wK·covK·key + wE·covE·energy)(s_i, s_{i+1}) + wS·covE·Σ_i shape(rel_i, target(i))`, an `int64` in units of 10⁻¹⁰ when weights are read as fractions (reported cost = `J / 10¹⁰`, 4 decimals).
`Distance(a, b)` is one transition under the `steady` weights (100, 80, 80) at coverage 100 with the **absolute** (unrescaled) energies — the same units as `J`, used by the Atlas neighbours. No `float` identifier appears in `cost.go` or `plan.go` (`TestSequencer_NoFloatInCost`).

**C137.4 Search.** **Exact, `n ≤ ExactMax (12)`: a suffix Held–Karp.** Index the Songs by their sorted id (so "smallest index" = lexicographically smallest id). `g(S, i)` = the minimum cost of the suffix that starts with Song `i` at position `p = n − |S| − 1` and then orders the set `S` of Songs that follow: `g(∅, i) = shape(i, n−1)` (infinite unless `i` is the required `Last`, when one is given) and `g(S, i) = shape(i, p) + min over j ∈ S of (trans(i, j) + g(S∖{j}, j))`, **ties broken by the smallest `j`**. The answer is `min over i of g(all∖{i}, i)` (restricted to `First` when given), ties by the smallest `i`. By induction every suffix is the lexicographically smallest optimal one, so the whole sequence is the lexicographically smallest optimum — a *forward* DP over (set, last) does not give that tie-break for free, which is why the suffix form is binding. States: `2^12·12 ≈ 49 k`. Because the candidate set never contains the input order, **the exact path is independent of the input order** (`TestPlan_ExactPermutationInvariant`: shuffled inputs of one set give the same order).
**Heuristic, `n > 12`:** a **beam search** (width `BeamWidth = 8`, partial sequences ranked by `(cost, id sequence)`, the best `3·BeamWidth` kept per depth, starting from every Song or from `First`), then a **local search** of 2-opt reversals followed by single-Song moves (scan order fixed, first improvement updates the working order, at most `MaxLocalPasses = 50` passes, endpoints never moved), run from the beam result **and from the input order** (when endpoints are given, the input order is first *repaired*: `First` moved to the front and `Last` to the end, the other Songs keeping their relative order); the better of the two is returned (ties lexicographic). Consequences, stated so no test claims more: (a) **same ordered input ⇒ same output** (`TestPlan_Deterministic`); (b) above 12 Songs a *shuffled* input may give a different result because the input order seeds one of the two searches, so shuffle-invariance is tested **only for n ≤ 12**; (c) **without endpoints `CostOutput ≤ CostInput`** (the input order is a candidate); (d) **with endpoints** the guarantee is only `CostOutput ≤ cost(repaired input)`, and `CostOutput` may exceed `CostInput` (the golden `arc, first = t5, last = t1` costs 9.2694 against an input of 8.9847). `Result.ImprovementPct` is signed (null when `CostInput = 0`) and the warning `endpoint_cost` is added when `CostOutput > CostInput`. `Exact` is true iff the DP ran. `record_unsequenced` never runs the planner (C139.2), so no flip-flop between `Exact` and heuristic results can reach a rule.
**Testability of the heuristic.** `Plan` takes its limits from an unexported `planOpts{exactMax int; forceHeuristic bool}` (the exported `Plan` passes `ExactMax`); tests use `planWith`. `TestSequencer_HeuristicGap`: 120 seeded random instances with `forceHeuristic` at `n ∈ {13, 14, 15}` (40 each, three strategies, no endpoints) with the exact DP, raised to `exactMax = 15`, as the oracle (`2^15·15 ≈ 490 k` states, milliseconds): mean gap **≤ 2 %**, worst **≤ 10 %**. `TestSequencer_Heuristic_LargeN`: `n = 30` and `n = 60` seeded sets, three strategies: byte-identical output on repeated runs and across goroutines, `CostOutput ≤ CostInput`, and `CostOutput ≤` the cost of a plain greedy nearest-neighbour order.

**C137.5 Explanation.** `Reason{Code string; Params map[string]int}`. Codes: position — `opener`, `closer`, `arc_peak` (the Song of highest relative energy when it sits within ±1 of index `round(0.7·(n−1))` under `arc`); tempo — `tempo_close` (cost ≤ 250 000), `tempo_octave` (the octave branch won and is cheaper than direct), `tempo_jump` (≥ 800 000); key — `key_same`, `key_relative`, `key_adjacent` (same letter, `dn` 1), `key_boost` (same letter, `dn` 2: a *deliberate lift*, allowed at cost 450 000 — costlier than a neighbour, never preferred over one), `key_clash` (≥ 700 000), `key_unknown`; energy — `energy_rise` (relative step ≥ +1000), `energy_fall` (≤ −1000), `energy_hold`; `no_features` (`missing: [bpm|key|energy]`). Warnings: `low_coverage {feature, known, total}` (coverage < 50 %), `predicted_energy {n}` (some energies have basis `predicted`), `heuristic` (not exact), `no_features {song}` (a Song with none of the three), `single_song`, `endpoint_cost`, `pending_features` (added by callers). **Rendered text** (`ExplainText`, golden `testdata/explain_text.golden.json`, 14 rows): opener ⇒ `"1. opens low: 96 BPM, key 8A, energy 0.00 (relative)"`; a transition ⇒ `"2. tempo 96 → 98 BPM (+2.1 %), key 8A → 8A (same), energy +0.25 — on the way up"`; the peak ⇒ `"5. peak: 108 BPM, key 9B, energy 1.00"`; the closer under `arc` ⇒ `"6. lands softly: energy 0.50 → 0.17"`; unknown ⇒ `"3. key unknown (neutral); no energy measurement (neutral)"`; `key_adjacent` ⇒ `"4. key 8A → 9A (neighbour on the wheel)"`; `key_boost` ⇒ `"4. key 8A → 10A (two steps apart: a deliberate lift, costlier than a neighbour)"` (the wording never says "better", because it costs more than adjacent). Texts never contain titles or ids.

**C137.6 Fixture and expected values (`TestPlan_Table`, the six-Song set T below; input order `t4, t6, t1, t5, t3, t2`).**

| id | BPM | energy | key |
|---|---|---|---|
| t1 | 96.0 | 0.30 | 8A |
| t2 | 98.0 | 0.45 | 8A |
| t3 | 100.0 | 0.55 | 9A |
| t4 | 104.0 | 0.70 | 9A |
| t5 | 108.0 | 0.90 | 9B |
| t6 | 102.0 | 0.40 | 8B |

Expected (exact DP; costs `J/10¹⁰`): **arc** ⇒ `t1, t2, t3, t4, t5, t6` (input 8.9847, output **5.5198**, improvement **38.6 %**); **ramp_up** ⇒ `t1, t6, t2, t3, t4, t5` (input 9.5761, output **2.5712**); **steady** ⇒ `t1, t6, t2, t3, t4, t5` (input 5.6840, output **3.3709**; a path and its reverse tie, the lexicographically smaller wins); **arc, first = t5, last = t1** ⇒ `t5, t4, t3, t2, t6, t1` (**9.2694**);
**arc, last = t5** ⇒ `t6, t2, t3, t1, t4, t5` (**7.5033**); **arc, first = t6** ⇒ `t6, t2, t1, t5, t4, t3` (**5.7820**). The endpoint rows are *constraints*, not improvements: the input order `t4, t6, t1, t5, t3, t2` violates `first = t5, last = t1`, so it is not a candidate and `arc, first = t5, last = t1` (9.2694) costs more than the input (8.9847, `improvement_pct` −3.2 %). Cost-function rows (`TestCost_Tempo`): (76.0, 152.0) ⇒ **300 000**; (76.0, 80.0) ⇒ **263 155**; (76.0, 95.0) ⇒ **1 000 000**; (120.0, 125.0) ⇒ **208 330**; (96.0, 108.0) ⇒ **625 000**; (76.0, 76.0) ⇒ **0**; (76.0, 84.0) ⇒ **526 315**; unknown ⇒ **500 000**; (`TestCost_Key`): 8A→8A **0**, 8A→8B **100 000**, 8A→9A **150 000**, 8A→10A **450 000**, 12A→1A **150 000**, 8A→9B **500 000**, 8A→11B **1 000 000**, unknown **500 000**.
`TestPlan_Symmetry` (tempo and key costs are symmetric for 1 000 random pairs), `TestPlan_UnknownFeatures` (two of six keys unknown ⇒ `covK = 66`, the key weight is `80·66`, unknown pairs cost 500 000), `TestPlan_AllUnknown` (no feature at all ⇒ order unchanged, warning `no_features`), `TestPlan_NeverWorse` (property: 200 seeded sets **without endpoints**, exact and forced-heuristic, `CostOutput ≤ CostInput`), `TestPlan_EndpointsMayCostMore` (the golden endpoint rows: `improvement_pct` −3.2 % for `first = t5, last = t1`, warning `endpoint_cost`, and the repaired-input bound), `TestPlan_Deterministic` (the same ordered input twice gives byte-identical `Result`, including `Digest`), `TestPlan_ExactPermutationInvariant` (n ≤ 12 only: shuffled inputs give the same order), `TestPlan_Endpoints` (errors: unknown id ⇒ `ErrEndpointMissing`, `First = Last` with `n > 1` ⇒ `ErrEndpointsConflict`), `TestSequencer_StrategiesGolden`, `TestExplainText_Golden`, `TestPerf_Sequencer` (n = 12 exact < 100 ms, n = 60 heuristic < 100 ms, env-scaled).

### C138. Sequencing in Records and the preview (`internal/sequence`, `internal/records`; amends L2 C121.3, C122.3, C124.2, C129)

```go
// internal/sequence
type Ref struct{ SongID string; TakeID *string }                        // the take is the slot's resolved Take (nil: the Song's subject)
func LoadFeatures(ctx context.Context, q store.Querier, refs []Ref) ([]sequencer.Song, map[string]FeatureBasis, error)   // one statement per table
type Service struct{ /* store */ }
func New(st *store.Store) *Service
func (s *Service) Suggest(ctx context.Context, viewer auth.Principal, in SuggestInput) (Suggestion, error)             // suggest_sequence; read-only transaction

type SuggestInput struct{ RecordID string; SongIDs []string; CrateID string; Strategy sequencer.Strategy; First, Last string }
type Suggestion struct {                                                // mm.sequence/1 (the same document the op stores, plus titles)
	Def string; Strategy string; InputOrder, Order []string; Steps []StepView
	Cost struct{ Input, Output string; ImprovementPct string }; Coverage Coverage; Exact bool
	Warnings []Warning; Digest string; Pending bool
}
// internal/records
type SeqSpec struct{ Strategy string; FirstSongID, LastSongID string }   // the SlotOp 'sequence' and propose_record.sequence payload
```

**C138.1 `suggest_sequence`** (read, free, `GET`, never writes): exactly one of `record_id`, `song_ids` (2..60), `crate_id` (a manual Song crate or a smart Song crate with ≤ 60 matches; else 422 `TOO_MANY_SONGS` with the count). `record_id` ⇒ the live slots in order, each slot's features from its **resolved Take** (`take_features`; a pinned slot uses its pinned Take, a floating one the Pick take, an empty slot the Song's `song_features`); `song_ids`/`crate_id` ⇒ each Song's `song_features` (its subject). Unknown ids ⇒ 422 `UNKNOWN_SONG`/`NOT_FOUND`; fewer than 2 Songs ⇒ 422 `SEQUENCE_TOO_SMALL`;
`strategy` default `arc`, unknown ⇒ 422 `UNKNOWN_STRATEGY`; `first_song_id`/`last_song_id` must be among the Songs (422 `SEQUENCE_ENDPOINT_NOT_IN_SET`). The response is `Suggestion` with Song and Take **titles injected at read** (`StepView = Step + {title, take_title}`); `pending` is true when the provenance queue holds a `track`/`draft` key for any involved Song (warning `pending_features`). `TestSuggest_EqualsApply` asserts the suggested order, cost and `digest` are byte-equal to what the `sequence` op stores for the same state.

**C138.2 The slot op `sequence`** (L2 C121.3 amendment): `SlotOp{Op: "sequence", Strategy, FirstSongID, LastSongID}`; all other `SlotOp` fields forbidden (422). Inside `EditSlots` (one write transaction, catch-up first): the op acts on the working list **as left by the previous ops** of the same call; it loads the features of the working list (C138.1 semantics for a Record), runs `sequencer.Plan`, rewrites the working order and records
`SequenceOutcome{ID, Strategy, Before, After, Result}`. At commit: one `record_sequences` row (`record_revision` = the revision the call was made against, `order_before_json`/`order_after_json` = slot ids, the explanation JSON `mm.sequence/1` of ids and numbers) and, per slot whose **final** position differs from its position before the *sequence op* (not before the call), one `slot_moved` row with `position` = the new position and `detail_json {"from": n, "by": "sequencer", "strategy": "arc", "sequence_id": "<id>"}`. A sequence that does not change the order writes the `record_sequences` row (the explanation is still useful: "already optimal") and **no** `slot_moved` row. Errors: 422 `SEQUENCE_NO_FEATURES` (no slot has any of tempo, key, energy), 422 `SEQUENCE_TOO_SMALL` (< 2 live slots), 422 `UNKNOWN_STRATEGY`, 422 `SEQUENCE_ENDPOINT_NOT_IN_SET`, 403 `HUMAN_ONLY` on a published Record for a non-human principal (the L2 rule, unchanged). A `sequence` on a `proposed` Record by a non-human principal sends it back to `draft` like every edit (L2 C120.1). The op never changes membership, pins, **sides** (the `side` labels are untouched: D-L2-5 gives sides no ordering rule) or `revision` semantics (one bump per call). A `sequence` run is a snapshot of the Picks of its moment: when a floating slot's Pick later changes (or the order is edited by hand) the stored explanation becomes `stale` (C138.5), and **`stale` is the only signal**: it does not re-raise `record_unsequenced`, which fires only for a Record that has never been sequenced.

**C138.3 `propose_record` amended** (L2 C129): body `{revision, rationale, sequence?: {strategy?, first_song_id?, last_song_id?}}`. With `sequence` present: in the **same transaction**, run the `sequence` step (C138.2) as the proposing principal on the `draft` Record, then the L2 `Propose` (needs ≥ 1 live slot: 422 `RECORD_EMPTY`, status `draft`: else 409 `INVALID_RECORD_STATE`); one `revision` bump for the whole call; the `proposed` change row gains `detail_json {"sequence_id": "<id>"}`. An error in the sequencing step (e.g. `SEQUENCE_NO_FEATURES`) fails the whole call and writes nothing (`TestProposeRecord_SequenceAtomic`). Without `sequence` the op is byte-for-byte the L2 behaviour (`TestProposeRecord_L2Unchanged`). L2's `records.Service.Propose(ctx, p, id, revision, rationale)` keeps its signature: F2 adds `ProposeWith(ctx, p, id, revision, rationale, seq *SeqSpec)` and `Propose` delegates with nil; `recordview.GetOptions.Include` accepts `sequence` (the op's default `liner,tapes,changes` is unchanged).

**C138.4 `ChangeText` (L2 C122.3) gains three rows** (golden 24 → 27): `slot_moved` with `detail.by = "sequencer"` ⇒ `"Slot moved {from} → {n} (sequenced: {strategy})"`; `proposed` with `detail.sequence_id` ⇒ `"Proposed for publishing (sequenced: {strategy})"`; `proposed` without ⇒ unchanged. `ChangeView` gains `sequence: {id, strategy} | null`. The web mirror `recordView.changeText` is tested against the same golden.

**C138.5 `get_record include=sequence`** (L2 C124.2): adds `sequence: SequenceExplanation | null` to `RecordView` — the **latest** `record_sequences` row of the Record rendered as in C138.1 (`StepView` with titles injected at read, the stored `explanation_json` + `exact`, `cost`, `actor` via `api.RefFor`, `at`, and `stale: true` when the Record's current live order differs from `order_after_json`). Default `include` of the op is unchanged (`liner,tapes,changes`); the SPA and agents ask for `sequence` explicitly. Mutating ops never emit it (they stay liner-less, L2 D-L2-27).

**C138.6 Privacy.** `explanation_json` contains ids, integers and codes only (no titles, no Muse text); `suggest_sequence` returns Song and Take **titles** (not Muse text) like `list_songs`. `TestSequence_NoMuseText` (canary Muse on every Song: no canary byte in `suggest_sequence`, in `record_sequences`, in the changelog rows or in `edit_record_slots`/`propose_record` responses).

### C139. Muse-aware next actions (`internal/nextactions`; D-L3-15…19)

```go
type Audience string                                                    // "agent" | "human" | "both"
type Action struct {
	Code string; Priority int                                            // 0..100
	Audience Audience; Subject Subject                                   // Subject{Kind: muse|song|record|taxonomy|library, ID *string}
	Text string; Hint *Hint; Evidence map[string]any                     // numbers and ids only
}
type Hint struct{ Op string; Args map[string]any }                      // a registry op (cost free) and its input fields (path parameters by name)
type Rule struct {
	Code string; Priority int; Audience Audience; MinScope auth.Scope
	Find func(ctx context.Context, q Reader, lim int) ([]Candidate, error)   // batch SQL, bounded
	Text func(c Candidate, n Names) string                                 // templates; n resolves Muse/Song/Record titles
}
type Filter struct{ SubjectKind, SubjectID string; MinPriority, Limit int; Cursor string; Audience Audience }
type Page struct{ Items []Action; Groups []Group; Total int; NextCursor *string; GeneratedAt string }
type Group struct{ Code string; More int }                               // items beyond PerCode for that code

func New(st *store.Store, muses MuseRefs, songview SongSummaries, sounds Unmapped, now func() time.Time) *Service
func (s *Service) List(ctx context.Context, viewer auth.Principal, f Filter) (Page, error)           // list_next_actions
func (s *Service) ForTake(ctx context.Context, viewer auth.Principal, trackID string, max int) ([]Action, error)   // /context
var Rules []Rule                                                         // the registry (extension move 3), pinned by testdata/rules_v1.golden.json
```
`trackctx` declares `type NextActionReader interface{ ForTake(ctx, p auth.Principal, trackID string, max int) ([]string, error) }` (strings already scope-filtered and rendered) and imports no L3 package; `app.Build` adapts `*nextactions.Service`.

**C139.1 Evaluation.** `List` evaluates every rule whose `Audience` admits the viewer (a human or device principal sees `human` + `both`, any other principal `agent` + `both`), collects at most `lim = 4·PerCode` candidates per rule (each rule's `Find` is **one batch statement**, indexed, no N+1; no `Find` runs the sequencer or any per-row query; live-Muse rules receive `LiveIDs` as a parameter, see C133), orders by `(Priority desc, Code asc, subject id asc)`, keeps at most `PerCode = 5` items per code (the remainder is counted in `Groups[code].More` and in `Total`), renders text and hints, then pages by an opaque keyset cursor. `Limit` 1..50 (default 20). Muse and Song titles are resolved once per call through `muses.Service.Refs` / the Song summaries (one statement each; Muse titles are cut to `MuseTitleRunes = 60`, the artist is never used). A rule with no candidates costs one query; the whole evaluation is a read-only transaction (`TestNextActions_GETNeverWrites`).

**C139.2 The rule catalogue (D-L3-19; every rule has a golden text row).** "Song state" and `songview.MuseSummaries` are L1's (C90.4); "strong" = the Song's subject take has human stars ≥ 4; `in Record` = a live slot in any non-hidden Record (L2).

| Code | Pri | Audience | Fires when | Text (template) | Hint |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `record_proposed_waiting` | 90 | human | a Record in status `proposed` | `Record "{title}" was proposed by {agent}: review it and publish` | – |
| `record_undecided_slots` | 80 | both | a live Record (draft, proposed or published) has ≥ 1 floating slot whose Pick source is `newest` or `stars` | `Record "{title}": {n} slot(s) play an undecided Pick — duel them, pin them, or freeze the Record (pin_all)` | `get_record {id, include: ["changes"]}` |
| `muse_record_candidate` | 75 | both | a live non-hidden Muse has ≥ 3 *strong* Songs that are in no Record | `Muse "{muse}" has {n} Songs rated 4★+ that are in no Record — build one: create_record, then propose_record with a sequence (arc)` | `list_songs {muse_id, min_stars: 4, in_record: false}` |
| `muse_unrendered` | 70 | agent | a live Muse has ≥ 1 draft Song, **no** Song with a take, and no queued job | `Muse "{muse}" has {n} Song(s), none rendered — estimate the first (the estimate is free)` | `estimate_generation {items: [{mode: "from_draft", draft_ids: [<first draft>]}]}` when the viewer has generate scope, else `get_muse {id}` |
| `taxonomy_proposals_open` | 70 | human | ≥ 1 open taxonomy proposal | `{n} taxonomy proposal(s) wait for your decision` | – |
| `song_duel` | 68 | both | a live Song has ≥ 2 eligible takes, < 3 counted human blind verdicts among them, and no Song-level pin | `Song "{title}" has {n} takes and {k} blind duel(s) — 3 decide the Pick; start one between the two newest` | `create_duel {a_track_id, b_track_id, blind: true}` |
| `muse_unjudged` | 65 | both | a live Muse has ≥ 1 Song with a take and **no** Song in state `judged` or `picked` | `Muse "{muse}" has {n} rendered Song(s) nobody has judged — ask the human to listen, or add a critique (add_comment)` | `get_muse {id}` |
| `muse_partial_angles` | 60 | agent | a live Muse has ≥ 1 Song with a take **and** ≥ 1 draft Song, no queued job | `Muse "{muse}": {angles} rendered, {angles} still draft — estimate {n} more` (angles listed ≤ 3, then `…`) | as `muse_unrendered` |
| `record_empty_slot` | 60 | both | a live slot resolves to no Take | `Record "{title}" slot {n} has no take yet — render the Song or remove the slot` | `get_record {id}` |
| `song_proposals_waiting` | 55 | both | a Song has ≥ 1 open Pick proposal | human: `Song "{title}": {n} agent proposal(s) wait — accept or dismiss`; agent: `Song "{title}": your proposal is waiting for the human` | `get_song {id}` |
| `song_tempo_mismatch` | 50 | agent | the Song's collapsed tempo row has `mismatch = true` and not `octave` | `Song "{title}": requested {r} BPM, measured about {m} — the tempo cue was probably ignored; write a stronger tempo cue into the style and estimate it (estimate_variant, mode style_edit, with your own patch.style)` | `get_song {id}` (not `estimate_variant`: `style_edit` needs a `patch.style` only the agent can author, so no executable `estimate_variant` hint exists) |
| `record_unsequenced` | 45 | both | a **draft** Record has ≥ `SequencePrecheckSlotsMin = 4` live slots, **no** `record_sequences` row ever, and ≥ 2 live slots whose Song has a tempo or energy feature (one batch statement; **the planner is never run** by this rule: previewing is free, so the rule does not need to know whether the order would change) | `Record "{title}" has not been sequenced — preview the order (suggest_sequence, arc)` | `suggest_sequence {record_id}` |
| `taxonomy_unmapped` | 35 | agent | a term is in `song_unmapped_terms` of ≥ 5 live Songs and in no open proposal | `The term "{term}" is unmapped in {n} Songs — propose a mapping (propose_taxonomy)` | `get_sound_taxonomy {unmapped: true}` |
| `song_no_muse` | 25 | agent | a live **recipe-known** Song (`recipe_known = true`) with a take has no Muse and no manual clear. The 7 originals and every reference Song are `recipe_known = false` and are **excluded**, because by design they have no Muse and there is no dismissal (the concept: each original has Muse: none) | `Song "{title}" has no Muse — if you know its inspiration: create_muse, then assign_muse_songs` | `get_song {id}` |
| `features_missing` | 20 | **human** | a live Song's subject is a **take** whose `take_features` has no measured tempo/key/energy while a master exists | `{n} Song(s) have no audio measurements yet — run "metamusic analyze" on the server` (library-level, one item; human audience because agents have no analyze operation) | – |

**Rule details.** (1) **First draft** (`muse_unrendered`, `muse_partial_angles`): the Muse's draft Songs ordered by angle rank (`remake…` 0, `alt-take…` 1, any other angle, including `long`, 2), then Song `created_at` ascending, then Song id; "the first draft" is the first of that order and `{angles}` lists up to 3 in the same order. (2) **Manual clear** (`song_no_muse`): the shipped schema marks an operator-cleared Muse with `songs.muse_source = 'manual'` and `muse_id IS NULL` (D-L1-37), so the rule is `muse_id IS NULL AND muse_source IS NOT 'manual'` (never set, never cleared); "recipe-known" is L2's `recipe_known` predicate (the subject take's recipe is known) — NA3 re-reads the merged L2 code for the shared SQL fragment. (3) **Hint validation:** a hint's `args` must satisfy the op's *required* fields and enum values, not only key names (`TestNextActions_HintsValidateAgainstOpenAPI` validates every emitted hint against the generated OpenAPI input schema: required properties present, types and enums valid; mode-specific requirements the schema cannot express, such as `estimate_variant` modes, are asserted per rule in `TestNextActions_Rules_Table`). (4) **Notifications:** next-action text is never placed in ntfy or any notification (C144.2); `record_proposed_waiting` is surfaced only here and by the existing L2 notification, which carries a count, never this text.

Texts use straight quotes, never raw ids; `{title}` values are the operator's own Song/Record titles (not Muse text) except `{muse}` (D-L3-16). Every `Hint.Op` exists in the registry with cost `free`, every `Args` key is an input field of that op, and no hint is emitted for an op the viewer's scope forbids (`TestNextActions_HintsAreFree`, `TestNextActions_HintsValidateAgainstOpenAPI`).

**C139.3 Library-level behaviour.** The library-level aggregate for a code that has more than `PerCode` candidates is the entry in `Groups` plus the total; the SPA What-next section prints it as `"{More} more like this"`. The list is a pure function of state: resolving the condition (rendering a Song, rating a take, creating a Record, mapping a term) removes the item on the next call; no dismissal exists.

**C139.4 `/context` integration (D-L3-17; amends M2a C35, M3 C81.1).** `trackctx.Build` assembles `next_actions` (the list follows `songwriting` and, after L2, `curation` in the serialised bundle) as: (1) the base rules in their existing order (rate, open fixes, untimed, truncated); (2) up to `ContextMuseMax = 2` Muse-aware strings **of priority ≥ `ContextMuseMinPriority = 50`** from `NextActionReader.ForTake(…, max)` where
`max = min(2, maxNextActions − len(base) − 1)` (the `-1` reserves the critique line) — `ForTake` evaluates only the rules whose subject is this take's Song, its Muse or a Record holding this take (`song_duel`, `song_proposals_waiting`, `song_tempo_mismatch`, `song_no_muse`, `muse_partial_angles`, `muse_unjudged`, `muse_record_candidate`, `record_undecided_slots`, `record_empty_slot`), in priority order; (3) the generation hint **with its existing condition evaluated after (2)** (`len(na)+2 <= maxNextActions`); (4) the critique line, always last. With no Song/Muse (a pre-L1 fixture, a take whose Song is queued, or a nil reader) the result is **byte-identical** to the M3 behaviour. `Fit` is unchanged (next actions are not trimmed; the cap is structural). Worked cases (`TestContext_NextActions_L3`): base = {fix} + a Song with a duel need + an unjudged Muse ⇒ `[fix, duel, unjudged, hint, critique]` (5); base = all four rules ⇒ `[rate, fix, untimed, truncated, critique]` (no room, nothing added, the existing test unchanged); base = {rate} + three eligible Muse-aware ⇒ the two highest priority are kept (`max = min(2, 5 − 1 − 1) = 2`), then the hint because `len(na)+2 = 3+2 ≤ 5` ⇒ `[rate, a, b, hint, critique]` (5); base = {rate, fix} + three eligible ⇒ `max = min(2, 5 − 2 − 1) = 2` ⇒ `[rate, fix, a, b, critique]` (5, hint dropped because `len(na)+2 = 6 > 5`); base of three ⇒ `max = 1`; base = {rate, fix} + only `song_no_muse` (25) eligible ⇒ below the floor, nothing added, the generation hint stays ⇒ `[rate, fix, hint, critique]` (the case that motivated the floor, pinned by `TestContext_NextActions_FloorKeepsHint`). **Ownership:** the assembly (steps 1–4, the `max` arithmetic, the floor) lives in `trackctx` and is implemented and tested by **NA1** (the single owner of `trackctx/nextactions.go`; F2 adds only the interface and the one call site in `Build`) against a *fake* `NextActionReader` (`TestContext_NextActions_L3`, `TestContext_NextActions_UnchangedWithoutSong`, `TestContext_NextActionsCap_StillFive`, `TestContext_Goldens_PreL3Unchanged`, `TestContext_NextActions_FloorKeepsHint`); **NA2** (once the core seven rules exist) re-runs the worked cases with the real reader on `FixtureL3` (`TestContext_NextActions_RealReader`).

### C140. Taxonomy proposals (`internal/sounds`; D-L3-20/21/22)

```go
type Mapping = MapEntry                                                // the shipped sounds.MapEntry{Term, Facet, Value, Op}; validation is the shipped validateMap (C93.2)
type Impact struct {
	TaxonomyVersion int; SongsScanned int; Truncated bool
	Gain, Loss []FacetCount                                            // [{Facet, Value, Songs}]
	UnmappedCleared []TermCount                                       // [{Term, Songs}]
}
type Proposal struct {
	ID, State string; BaseVersion int; Rationale string; Mappings []Mapping; Impact Impact
	ProposedBy PrincipalRef; CreatedAt string; DecidedAt, DecidedBy *string; DecidedNote *string
	Accepted, Skipped json.RawMessage; AppliedVersion *int; Reused bool
}
type Decision struct{ Decision string /* accept | reject */; AcceptMappings []int; Note string }
type DecideResult struct{ Proposal Proposal; Applied *MapResult; Skipped []Skipped }                          // Skipped{Index, Reason}

func (s *Service) Propose(ctx context.Context, p auth.Principal, rationale string, ms []Mapping) (Proposal, error)
func (s *Service) ListProposals(ctx context.Context, f ProposalFilter) (ProposalPage, error)
func (s *Service) Decide(ctx context.Context, p auth.Principal, id string, d Decision) (DecideResult, error)
func (s *Service) Impact(ctx context.Context, ms []Mapping) (Impact, error)                                       // dry run, read-only
```

**C140.1 `Propose`** (scope write; `ActsAsHuman()` principals ⇒ 403 `HUMAN_ONLY` hint `use map_sound_term`): `rationale` 1..500 (trimmed); `mappings` 1..50 validated exactly as `Map` (C93.2: folded terms, facet set of five, value patterns; errors `INVALID_TERM`, `FACET_NOT_MAPPABLE`, 422 `MAPPING_NOT_EFFECTIVE` for a `remove` that is not effective) with these differences: an `add` that is **already effective** is dropped and reported (`unchanged`); nothing left ⇒ 422 `NOTHING_TO_PROPOSE`; two entries for one `(term, facet, value)` ⇒ 422 `DUPLICATE_MAPPING` (a repeated identical entry is tolerated by `Map`, refused by a proposal; add and remove of the same triple is `Map`'s `CONFLICTING_MAPPINGS`). Limits: ≤ `ProposalMaxOpenPerPrincipal = 10` open per principal and `ProposalMaxOpen = 50` overall (409 `TAXONOMY_PROPOSAL_LIMIT` with `details.scope`). An open proposal of the same principal with the same canonical mapping set (`mappings_digest`) is returned unchanged with `reused: true` (200, no row). Otherwise one row, state `open`, `base_version` = the current taxonomy version, `impact_json` = `Impact(mappings)` snapshot; 201. Idempotency-Key supported (`create`).

**C140.2 `Impact`** (read-only, bounded): builds the hypothetical index (`BuildIndex` with the mappings applied on top of the current effective rows), then, for up to `ImpactMaxSongs` live Songs (newest first; `Truncated` when more exist), re-parses the style of the Song's **representative subject** (the exact input of `ComputeSongTx`) and counts, per `(facet, value)`, the Songs that would **gain** or **lose** that row, plus the unmapped terms a mapping would clear (`song_unmapped_terms` ∩ the added phrases, counted from the cache). Pure arithmetic over the same `Parse`; `TestImpact_EqualsRecompute` applies the mappings through `Map` on a scratch copy and asserts the real recompute changed exactly the predicted rows.

**C140.3 `Decide`** (scope write, `ActsAsHuman()` else 403 `HUMAN_ONLY` before any read, with a hint as `picks/service.go` does): the proposal must be `open` (409 `TAXONOMY_PROPOSAL_CLOSED`); `note` ≤ 300. **reject** ⇒ state `rejected`, `decided_*`. **accept** ⇒ `accept_mappings` absent = all, else a set of distinct valid indices (422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`). One writer transaction (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`): re-read the proposal, re-validate each accepted mapping against the effective index *now* (an `add` already effective or a `remove` no longer effective is `skipped`, `reason: "stale"`); none remaining ⇒ the row becomes `expired` (`decided_by = 'system:sounds'`), the transaction **commits**, and `Decide` returns `(DecideResult{Proposal: expired}, ErrTaxonomyStale)` — the handler maps it to 409 `TAXONOMY_STALE` and must not roll anything back (`TestDecide_ExpiredRowPersists` reads the row after the 409); otherwise `sounds.applyTx` inserts **one extension version** attributed to the human with note `proposal <id>` (≤ 50 mappings) and the proposal row becomes `accepted` (`accepted_json`, `skipped_json`, `applied_version`) in the **same** transaction; commit. After the commit `Decide` runs `sounds.recompute` (the batched loop `Map` uses: every stale subject is recomputed before the call answers) — a crash between the commit and the loop leaves a version whose subjects are SQL-stale, which the startup sweep finishes exactly as it does for `map_sound_term` today. **Feature refresh mechanism (named, not implied):** `sounds` cannot import `features`, so `sounds.Service.SetRecomputeHook` (C140.6) is called inside each recompute transaction with the Song ids the batch rewrote and `app` wires it to `features.ComputeTx`; `song_features.family/vocal/era/mood/language` therefore change atomically with `song_sounds`, before `Decide` returns (`TestDecide_RefreshesFeaturesInPlace`; the hook is also what a human `map_sound_term` uses). No queue row is written and nothing waits for a sweep.

**C140.4 `map_sound_term` amendment (D-L3-22).** `Meta.MCP = "exclude:human-judgment"`; handler first statement: a non-`ActsAsHuman` principal ⇒ 403 `HUMAN_ONLY` with hint `use propose_taxonomy`; no state change, audit row with status 403 (the `pin_pick` pattern). The L1 parity case moves to REST-only tests; `TestMap_AgentAllowed` becomes `TestMap_AgentRefused` (and `TestMap_DeviceAllowed`). The frozen `Meta` is F2's and the guard and tests are TP2's, **only after the D-L3-22 box is ticked**; until then agents keep `map_sound_term` (the default) and nothing here changes it.

**C140.5 Visibility.** `rationale` and agent-authored `term`s are stored and returned by `list_taxonomy_proposals`/`get_sound_taxonomy` by design (D-L1 canary rule): they appear **only** there and never in logs, audit text, job events, notifications, exports or `problem+json` (canaries, C144).

**C140.6 `sounds` refactors (behaviour-preserving, guarded by the unchanged `TestMap_*` and `TestCompute_*`; D-L3-41).** F1 exports `SubjectOf(ctx, q querier, songID string) (Subject, error)` with `Subject{Kind string // take, draft or none; ID string}` (the unexported `loadSubject` calls it). F2 adds `Changes.SongIDs []string` (the Songs a recompute batch rewrote) and `Service.SetRecomputeHook(func(ctx context.Context, tx *sql.Tx, env Env, songIDs []string) error)` (nil-safe; stored, not yet called). TP1 splits `Map` into
`validateMap` → `applyTx(ctx, tx, p, entries, note) (MapResult, bool, error)` (the version and term inserts, `Unchanged` counting, `MAPPING_NOT_EFFECTIVE`) → commit → `recompute(ctx, p) (Changes, error)` and calls the hook inside every `recomputeBatch` transaction (an error aborts the batch). `Service` stays safe for concurrent use (`mu`).

### C141. The Song Atlas and the requested-vs-got layer (`internal/atlas`; D-L3-14, 24–30, 27–28)

```go
type Kind string                                                        // "numeric" | "categorical"
type AxisDef struct {
	Name string; Kind Kind; Unit, Scale string                          // Scale "linear" | "log2"
	Column string                                                       // song_features column
	BasisColumn string                                                  // "bpm_basis" | "energy_basis" | "" ; bpm_requested/bpm_measured carry a fixed basis
	Min, Max int                                                        // numeric domain in the column's fixed-point units
	Order []string                                                      // categorical: the fixed category order
}
var Axes []AxisDef                                                      // the registry (extension move 2), pinned by testdata/axes_v1.golden.json

type Query struct {
	X, Y, ColorBy string; Filter songs.ListFilter; Dim bool
	NearSongID string; K int; Limit int; Cursor string
}
type Atlas struct { /* C142 shape */ }
func New(st *store.Store, now func() time.Time) *Service
func (s *Service) Get(ctx context.Context, viewer auth.Principal, q Query) (Atlas, error)          // read-only transaction; never writes
func (s *Service) Adherence(ctx context.Context, q AdherenceQuery) (Adherence, error)
func Layout(pts []Input, x, y AxisDef) []XY                              // pure, integer-only; C141.2
func (s *Service) AxisInfos(...)                                        // for tests
```

**C141.1 Axes (D-L3-24).** Numeric (fixed-point domains): `tempo` (`bpm_x100`, `log2`, 6000–20000, "BPM"), `bpm_requested` (`bpm_requested_x100`, same scale), `bpm_measured` (`bpm_measured_x100`, same scale), `energy` (`energy_x10000`, linear, 0–10000), `duration` (`duration_s`, linear, 60–360 s, in centiseconds). Categorical: `family`, `vocal`, `era`, `mood`, `language` (the five primary Sound columns of `song_features`; orders = the L1 seed value lists: `family` in the L1 C93.1 order, `vocal` male female duet choir rap instrumental, `era` 50s…20s, `mood` upbeat chill melancholic romantic dark dreamy energetic nostalgic aggressive warm, `language` en it es fr de pt) and `key` (the 24 Camelot codes `1A, 1B, 2A, …, 12B`). A categorical axis shows only the categories **present** in the scope (fixed order) plus a trailing `(none)` band when a Song lacks the value. Basis codes of a plotted numeric value: `m` measured, `d` declared, `l` plan, `p` predicted (`bpm_requested`: `d`/`l`; `bpm_measured`: `m`; `duration`: `m`).

**C141.2 Layout (`layout.go`, integer-only, D-L3-29).**
1. *Normalise.* Numeric `u ∈ [0, 65536]` (Q16): `tempo`-family axes `u = (ilog2q16(bpm_x100) − ilog2q16(100) − L60)·65536 / (L200 − L60)` clamped (so fractional BPM is kept: no integer division of `bpm_x100` by 100), with `ilog2q16` the bit-by-bit fixed-point base-2 logarithm (16 fractional bits) and `L60`, `L200` committed constants (`TestIlog2_AgainstMath`: within 2 LSB of `math.Log2` for 6000…20000); `energy` `u = e·65536/10000`; `duration` `u = (clamp(cs,6000,36000) − 6000)·65536/30000`. Categorical: band `i` of `n` ⇒ `[i·65536/n, (i+1)·65536/n]`.
2. *Group.* Points with the same **cell** share a spiral: numeric × numeric — `(⌊ux·QuantNumeric/65536⌋, ⌊uy·QuantNumeric/65536⌋)` with `QuantNumeric = 128` (the one value, as in C133 and D-L3-29; a cell is 1/128 of each axis, about 1.4 BPM wide near 100 BPM and 0.008 of the energy range — **on the real library many Songs share a cell**, see the dense-stack note after the worked values); categorical × categorical — the band pair; mixed — `(band, ⌊u·QuantMixed/65536⌋)` with `QuantMixed = 64`.
3. *Place.* Members of a group sorted by song id, index `k` of `m`. numeric × numeric: position = the exact `(ux, uy)` of the member **plus** `spiral(k)·R0·√k` with `R0 = 0.0035` (Q16 229; k = 0 is not offset). Categorical × categorical: the band-cell centre plus `(cosθ_k·ρ_k·0.45·bw, sinθ_k·ρ_k·0.45·bh)` with `ρ_k = √((k+½)/m)` (m = 1 ⇒ 0) — a sunflower inside the cell. Mixed: the numeric coordinate exact, the categorical coordinate `band_lo + ((k+½)/m)·0.9·bw + 0.05·bw` (a horizontal strip). `θ_k = k·137.508°` taken as `⌊k·137508/1000⌋ mod 360` into a **committed 360-entry unit-circle table in Q16** (`spiral_table.go`, checked by `TestSpiralTable_AgainstMath` within 1 LSB; `layout.go` calls no `math.` function and contains no `float32`/`float64`, `TestAtlas_NoFloatInLayout`); `isqrt` is an integer square root.
4. *Output.* Coordinates in [0, 1], `round(q16·10000/65536)/10000` (4 decimals), y increasing upward (the client flips). Identical inputs give byte-identical output on amd64 and arm64; goldens `testdata/layout_*.golden.json` (six: tempo×energy with duplicates, family×vocal, requested×got, key×energy, a single point, an empty set).
Worked values (`TestLayout_Normalise`): 60 BPM ⇒ 0.0000; 200 BPM ⇒ 1.0000; 120 BPM ⇒ **0.5757**; 76 BPM ⇒ **0.1963**; energy 0.65 ⇒ **0.6500**; duration 210 s ⇒ **0.5000**; one categorical axis with 3 present categories ⇒ band centres 0.1667, 0.5000, 0.8333.
**Dense stacks are the normal case on the real library, not an edge case.** The ~456 draft Songs carry only ~59 distinct BPM values, and their predicted energy is a function of three facets (tempo band, family, mood), so there are only a few distinct y-values: the default tempo × energy map is a set of **dense vertical stacks**, and the spiral resolves *stacks*, not "almost identical values". `TestLayout_DenseStacks` pins this with `testdata/layout_dense.golden.json`: a seeded set of 456 points over 59 x-values with y from `PredictEnergy` over seeded facets (about a dozen distinct y); asserts all 456 output positions are pairwise distinct at 4 decimals, every point stays inside [0, 1], no point is farther than 0.03 from its exact `(ux, uy)`, and the bytes equal the golden. The UI says so (C145.2 legend text): predicted energy partly encodes the tempo band, so a stack is not a measurement.

**C141.3 `Get`.** One read-only transaction (`TestGETNeverWritesDB_L3`). (1) **Scope**: live (non-hidden, non-retired, non-archived) Songs joined to `song_features`, newest first, capped at `MaxScope` (`truncated`, `scope_total`); with filters and `Dim = false` the scope is the filtered set, with `Dim = true` it is the unfiltered scope and non-matching points carry `h:0` (matching `h:1`). The filter is the **shared Song vocabulary** (`songs.ListFilter`, L1 C90.7 + L2 C125.3: `muse_id`, `no_muse`, `angle`, `state`, `queued`, `session_id`, `sound`, `tempo_mismatch`, `has_pick`, `pick_decided`, `has_open_proposals`, `draft_id`, `track_id`, `include_hidden`, `include_archived`, `q`, `unheard`, `min_duration_s`, `max_duration_s`, `min_stars`, `unrated`, `tag`, `lossy`, `recipe_known`, `in_record`, `crate_id`), the embedded `api.SongFilterIn` shared by `list_songs`, `search_sounds` and `get_song_atlas` and converted by the same code (the handler expands `crate_id` through `crates.ExpandSongs`; `atlas` receives a plain `songs.ListFilter`, D-L3-44) (`TestAtlas_FilterEqualsListSongs`: for ten filters the Atlas `totals.scope` equals `list_songs.total`; `crate_id` of a smart Crate selects the same Songs as the Crate). (2) **Plot**: a numeric axis needs a non-NULL value (else `missing`); a categorical axis puts a missing value in `(none)`. (3) **Layout and token.** One scope query returns, for every in-scope Song in id order, the *row tuple* `(id, song_features.digest, h, st, pk, rec, muse id, the colour value of color_by)` — everything that decides membership, order, coordinates, flags or overlay colour. `layout_token = sha256(x, y, filter canonical JSON, dim, taxonomy_version, the ordered row tuples)`. The token is computed from the **actual scope on every request** (≤ 5 000 rows, microseconds to hash), so a rating or star, a listening event (`unheard`), job state (`queued`, `state`), a tag, session membership, `lossy`, an open proposal, a Record slot, a Crate edit or a Muse assignment that changes the scope, a flag, a colour or a point changes the token. No "data version" tuple exists (the first draft's tuple omitted ratings, listening events, job state, tags, session membership, `lossy` and proposals). The layout is a pure function of the axes and the point values, and is cached in memory **keyed by `layout_token`**: `LayoutCacheEntries = 4` layouts; `LayoutCacheTTL = 60 s` is only a memory bound, never a freshness rule, because a changed scope has a different token and misses the cache. (4) **Page**: points ordered by song id, `Limit` 1..`MaxPage` (default `DefaultPage` for a human principal, `AgentDefaultPage = 100` for any other principal; the default depends on the principal kind, never on the transport, so REST and MCP agree), cursor = `layout_token` + last id (a different token ⇒ 409 `ATLAS_STALE`; the client restarts). The 90 000-byte cap is enforced in Go by cutting the page at a whole point. (5) **Overlay** `color_by` ∈ `none, muse, state, pick, family, vocal, era, mood, language, key, record`: `c` per point, `legend` entries `{c, value, count}` ordered by count desc then value, palette index = rank, ranks ≥ 12 share index 12 ("other"); `muse` ⇒ `value` is the Muse **id**, `mi` indexes the response's `muses` id dictionary (≤ 12 entries + other; **no Muse text anywhere**, names come from `list_muses`); `state` ⇒ `draft rendered judged picked` (L1 C90.4), `pick` ⇒ `none newest stars ladder pinned`, `record` ⇒ `none draft_record published_record` (L2 live slots). (6) **Neighbours** (demoted, D-L3-35): `near_song_id` (+`k` ≤ 25, default 8, first page only) ⇒ the Songs of the scope nearest by `sequencer.Distance` over their `song_features` (absolute energy, no rescale; Songs with no feature at all skipped), `{id, title, distance (4 dec), parts {tempo, key, energy}}`; unknown Song ⇒ 404 `NOT_FOUND`. (7) **Totals**: `{scope, scope_total, truncated, plotted, hit, missing {x, y}, basis {x: {m, d, l, p}, y: {…}}}` — the honesty layer the SPA prints ("456 plotted · 13 measured · rest declared/predicted"). `pending` = a dirty `track`/`draft` key exists for any scope Song. Page cap 90 000 B, `title` ≤ `TitleRunes`.

**C141.4 Requested vs got (`get_adherence`; absorbs M6 @@C138.2, D-L3-27/28).** Unit = a live Song's **subject take** (the Pick take) whose `song_features` has both `bpm_requested_x100` and `bpm_measured_x100`. **Error formula (verbatim from M6, integer form):** with `req`, `got` in centi-BPM, `e1 = |got − req|·10000/req`, `eh = |got − 2·req|·10000/(2·req)` (the estimate is double the request), `ed = |2·got − req|·10000/req` (the estimate is half the request); `err = min(e1, eh, ed)` (units of 0.01 %), `within_5pct = err ≤ 500`. **`octave` has one definition in all of L3:** the `bpm_octave` flag of `song_features`, i.e. L1 `sounds.CompareTempo`'s `octave` (measured within 6 % of 2× or ½× the requested BPM, D-L3-5); it is what the position rule, the Atlas guide lines, the tooltips and `get_adherence` all report. It is **not** derived from the error (`err < e1` would call "asked 72, got 100" an octave while the features say it is not). The error `err` is reported as-is (a number, not a verdict). Pinned rows (`TestAdherence_Formula`): asked 72, got 144 ⇒ **0.00 %, octave true**; asked 72, got 88 ⇒ **22.22 %, octave false**; asked 72, got 100 ⇒ **30.55 %, octave false** (the minimum is the half-request comparison, which the retired `err < e1` rule would have mislabelled an octave); asked 120, got 126 ⇒ **5.00 %, within**; asked 120, got 127 ⇒ 5.83 %, not within; asked 76, got 38 ⇒ 0.00 %, octave true. Duration: `ratio = duration_s / duration_expected_s` (3 decimals) when both exist (`duration_expected_s` = `scores.bars × seconds_per_bar`).
`Adherence{dims_available: ["bpm","duration"] (those with ≥ 1 row), coverage {songs, with_pair, with_duration}, summary {n, within_5pct_share, octave_share, median_error_pct}, by_facet: [{facet, value, n, bpm {n, within_5pct_share, median_error_pct, octave_share, mean_requested, mean_got} | null, duration {n, median_ratio} | null}] (facets `family vocal era mood language`, `n ≥ min_n` default 3), items: [{song_id, take_id, title, requested_bpm, measured_bpm, error_pct, octave, duration_ratio | null}] (ordered by `error_pct desc, song_id`, keyset cursor, `limit` 1..100 (50))}`; filters `facet`+`value`, `min_error_pct`, `octave` (bool). Medians: sorted `err`, odd n the middle, even n the mean of the two middle values rounded half up; shares are 4-decimal ratios; means 1 decimal. `TestAdherence_ByFacet` (golden), `TestAdherence_NoPairs` (no measurements ⇒ `dims_available: []`, empty lists, 200), `TestAdherence_UsesPickTake` (a Song with two takes counts once, via its Pick).

### C142. Operations (types in `internal/api/types_l3.go`; JSON snake_case)

Legend (M2a C38): **C** = `idempotency: create`; **H** = `rate: heavy`; all L3 operations are `cost: free` (`TestL3_NoSpendOps`, D-L3-18). `exclude:human-judgment` ops keep a UI route (the SPA calls them) but have no MCP face; the registry-derived "agent does every UI action" set skips them by the existing rule
(`pin_pick` and `dismiss_pick_proposal` are the shipped precedent). `propose_taxonomy` is an agent-face op like `propose_pick`: its `UI` is a `none:` reason (D-L3-38).

| OperationID | Method + path | Scope | Cost | MCP | UI | Flags | Output |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `suggest_sequence` | GET `/api/sequence` | read | free | generic | `/records/:id` | | `Suggestion` (C138.1) |
| `list_next_actions` | GET `/api/next-actions` | read | free | tool | `/` | | `NextActionsPage` |
| `propose_taxonomy` | POST `/api/taxonomy-proposals` | write | free | tool | `none:agent-face (a human maps terms directly with map_sound_term)` | C, H | 201/200 `TaxonomyProposal` |
| `list_taxonomy_proposals` | GET `/api/taxonomy-proposals` | read | free | generic | `/sounds` | | `{items: TaxonomyProposal[], next_cursor, total}` |
| `decide_taxonomy_proposal` | POST `/api/taxonomy-proposals/{id}/decision` | write | free | `exclude:human-judgment` | `/sounds` | | `DecideResult` |
| `get_song_atlas` | GET `/api/atlas` | read | free | generic | `/atlas` | | `Atlas` (C141.3) |
| `get_adherence` | GET `/api/atlas/adherence` | read | free | generic | `/atlas` | | `Adherence` (C141.4) |

7 operations: 2 first-class tools, 4 generic, 1 excluded (`TestOps_RegisteredWithMetaL3`). After L3 the registry holds 109 + 17 (L2) + 7 = **133** operations; F2 re-pins `TestRegistry_OpCount` in **both** pins (`ops_m3_test.go`, `ops_m2c_test.go`) at 133. Agent counterparts of the human-judgment op: `propose_taxonomy` and
`list_taxonomy_proposals` (to read the outcome). **`tools/list`:** F2 measures the compact `tools/list` on top of L2 rev 3's re-baselined constant with the 2 new tools **and** the grown `propose_record`/`get_record` schemas, sets `toolsCeilingBytes` to the measured value rounded up (a committed constant with a one-line justification, never an
`-update` side effect) and re-baselines `internal/mcpgen/testdata/tools_list.golden.json`; if the two L3 tools alone exceed **4 000 compact bytes** it demotes `propose_taxonomy` to generic and re-measures; beyond that it stops and asks the operator (D-M3-36 pattern; `TestToolsList_L3Budget` mirrors L1's budget test and prints the measurement).
**Body limits** (`MaxBodyBytes`): `propose_taxonomy` and `decide_taxonomy_proposal` are `bodySmall` (64 KiB; ≤ 50 mappings); `edit_record_slots`/`propose_record` keep their L2 limits (`TestOps_BodyLimitsL3`).

**Inputs:**
- `suggest_sequence`: exactly one of `record_id`, `song_id` (explode, 2..60), `crate_id`; `strategy` (`arc` default, `ramp_up`, `steady`); `first_song_id`, `last_song_id`.
- `list_next_actions`: `subject_kind` (`muse`, `song`, `record`, `taxonomy`, `library`), `subject_id`, `min_priority` 0..100, `audience` (`agent`, `human`, `both`; default by principal), `limit` 1..50 (20), `cursor`.
- `propose_taxonomy`: `IdemHeader` + `{rationale: string 1..500, mappings: [{term, facet, value, op: add or remove}] (1..50)}`.
- `list_taxonomy_proposals`: `state` (explode: open, accepted, rejected, expired), `include_impact` (bool: recompute the impact now instead of the snapshot; at most 10 proposals are recomputed per call), `limit` 1..100 (50), `cursor`.
- `decide_taxonomy_proposal`: path `id`; `{decision: accept or reject, accept_mappings?: int[0..49], note?: string 1..300}`.
- `get_song_atlas`: `x` (default `tempo`), `y` (default `energy`), `color_by` (default `none`), `dim` (bool), `near_song_id`, `k` 1..25, `limit` 1..600 (400 for human principals, 100 for others), `cursor`, **plus the embedded `SongFilterIn`** (with L2's added predicates, C141.3).
- `get_adherence`: `facet`, `value`, `min_n` 1..50 (3), `min_error_pct`, `octave` (bool), `limit` 1..100 (50), `cursor`.
- **Amended existing inputs (no new ops; types and `Meta` by F2, handler bodies by the named lane):** `edit_record_slots` — `SlotOp.op` enum gains `sequence` with `strategy`, `first_song_id`, `last_song_id` (SR); `propose_record` — body gains `sequence?: {strategy?, first_song_id?, last_song_id?}` (SR); `get_record` — `include` gains `sequence` (SR);
  `list_songs`/`search_sounds` — unchanged parameters; `get_song` — output gains `features` (AD); `get_track_analysis` and `/context.perception` — outputs gain `key`, `energy` (E1; shapes by F2). **`get_stats` is not amended** (D-L3-33).

**Shapes** (abridged; full in `types_l3.go`; principals through `api.RefFor`; **no Muse text outside `NextAction.text`**):
```
NextAction        {code, priority, audience, subject: {kind, id|null}, text, hint: {op, args}|null, evidence}
NextActionsPage   {items: NextAction[], groups: [{code, more}], total, next_cursor|null, generated_at}
TaxonomyProposal  {id, state, base_version, rationale, mappings: [{term, facet, value, op}], impact: {taxonomy_version, songs_scanned, truncated,
                   gain: [{facet, value, songs}], loss: [...], unmapped_cleared: [{term, songs}]}, proposed_by (RefFor), created_at,
                   decided_at|null, decided_by (RefFor)|null, decided_note|null, accepted: int[]|null, skipped: [{index, reason}]|null,
                   applied_version|null, reused}
DecideResult      {proposal: TaxonomyProposal, applied: MapResult|null, skipped: [{index, reason}]}
Suggestion        (C138.1)   SequenceExplanation (C138.5)
FeaturesBlock     {tempo: {bpm|null, basis|null, requested_bpm|null, measured_bpm|null, octave}, energy: {value|null, basis|null},
                   key: {camelot|null, name|null, basis|null}, duration_ratio|null, subject: {kind, id, note|null}, pending}         // get_song.features
Atlas             {def: "mm.atlas/1", generated_at, taxonomy_version, layout_token, pending, axes: {x: AxisInfo, y: AxisInfo}, guides: [{kind}],
                   color_by, legend: [{c, value, count}], muses: string[] (ids; only for color_by=muse),
                   totals: {scope, scope_total, truncated, plotted, hit, missing: {x, y}, basis: {x: {m, d, l, p}, y: {m, d, l, p}}},
                   points: [{id, t, x, y, c, mi?, st, pk, xb?, yb?, h?}], neighbors?: [{id, title, distance, parts: {tempo, key, energy}}], next_cursor}
AxisInfo          {name, kind, unit?, scale?, min?, max?, categories?: [{value, lo, hi, count}]}
Adherence         (C141.4)
```
`DecideResult` has no `pending` field (the recompute and the feature refresh finish before it returns, C140.3). Point codes: `st` 0 draft · 1 rendered · 2 judged · 3 picked; `pk` 0 none · 1 newest · 2 stars · 3 ladder · 4 pinned; `xb`/`yb` ∈ `m d l p` (omitted on categorical axes); `h` 0/1 only with `dim`.
**Error codes (new, stable):** 404 `NOT_FOUND` (unknown record/crate/song/proposal ids — message carries only the id); 409 `ATLAS_STALE`, `TAXONOMY_PROPOSAL_CLOSED`, `TAXONOMY_STALE`, `TAXONOMY_PROPOSAL_LIMIT`; 403 `HUMAN_ONLY` (decide, `map_sound_term`, hint texts per C140); 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `UNKNOWN_AXIS`, `AXIS_PAIR_INVALID`, `UNKNOWN_COLOR_BY`,
`UNKNOWN_FACET` (L1, `songs/filter.go`), `INVALID_CURSOR`, `UNKNOWN_STRATEGY`, `SEQUENCE_TOO_SMALL`, `SEQUENCE_NO_FEATURES`, `SEQUENCE_ENDPOINT_NOT_IN_SET`, `TOO_MANY_SONGS`, `UNKNOWN_SONG`, `NOTHING_TO_PROPOSE`, `DUPLICATE_MAPPING`, and the shipped `sounds` codes reused by proposals: `INVALID_TERM`, `FACET_NOT_MAPPABLE`, `INVALID_FACET`, `INVALID_VALUE`, `INVALID_OP`,
